# OpenAI Web Search Tool — Complete Guide

## Overview

The web search tool lets models access current internet information and return answers with sourced citations. It's available via the **Responses API** (primary) and **Chat Completions API** (limited).

## Three Types of Web Search

1. **Non-reasoning web search** — The model sends the query to the search tool and returns results directly. Fast, ideal for quick lookups.
2. **Agentic search with reasoning models** — The model manages the search process as part of its chain of thought, analyzing results and deciding whether to continue searching. Higher latency but suited for complex workflows.
3. **Deep research** — Extended, agent-driven investigation using models like `o3-deep-research`, `o4-mini-deep-research`, or `gpt-5` with high reasoning. Can run for minutes and is best used with background mode.

## API Request Format

### Responses API (Recommended)

```python
from openai import OpenAI
client = OpenAI()

response = client.responses.create(
    model="gpt-5",
    tools=[{"type": "web_search"}],
    input="What was a positive news story from today?"
)

print(response.output_text)
```

```javascript
import OpenAI from "openai";
const client = new OpenAI();

const response = await client.responses.create({
    model: "gpt-5",
    tools: [{ type: "web_search" }],
    input: "What was a positive news story from today?",
});

console.log(response.output_text);
```

```bash
curl "https://api.openai.com/v1/responses" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY" \
    -d '{
        "model": "gpt-5",
        "tools": [{"type": "web_search"}],
        "input": "what was a positive news story from today?"
    }'
```

### Chat Completions API

Requires specialized models and always retrieves from the web before responding:

```python
completion = client.chat.completions.create(
    model="gpt-4o-search-preview",
    web_search_options={},
    messages=[{
        "role": "user",
        "content": "What was a positive news story from today?",
    }],
)
```

## Parameters

### `tools` Configuration (Responses API)

The tool object in the `tools` array accepts:

| Parameter | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| `type` | `"web_search"` (GA) or `"web_search_preview"` (earlier version) |
| `user_location` | Approximate geographic location to refine results |
| `filters` | Domain filtering with `allowed_domains` (up to 100 URLs) |
| `external_web_access` | Boolean; `false` for offline/cache-only mode (default `true`) |

### `user_location` Parameter

Refines results based on geography with these fields:

- **`type`**: `"approximate"`
- **`country`**: Two-letter ISO country code (e.g., `"GB"`, `"US"`)
- **`city`**: Free text string (e.g., `"London"`)
- **`region`**: Free text string (e.g., `"London"`, `"Minnesota"`)
- **`timezone`**: IANA timezone (e.g., `"America/Chicago"`)

```python
tools=[{
    "type": "web_search",
    "user_location": {
        "type": "approximate",
        "country": "GB",
        "city": "London",
        "region": "London",
    }
}]
```

> Note: "user location is not supported for deep research models using web search."

### Domain Filtering (`filters`)

Limits results to specific domains (Responses API only). Omit HTTP/HTTPS prefix; subdomains are included.

```python
tools=[{
    "type": "web_search",
    "filters": {
        "allowed_domains": [
            "pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov",
            "clinicaltrials.gov",
            "www.who.int",
            "www.cdc.gov",
            "www.fda.gov"
        ]
    }
}]
```

### Live Internet Access Control

```python
tools=[{ "type": "web_search", "external_web_access": False }]
```

The `web_search_preview` variant ignores this parameter and always uses live access.

### Sources

To retrieve all URLs consulted (not just cited), use the `include` parameter:

```python
include=["web_search_call.action.sources"]
```

"Unlike inline citations, which show only the most relevant references, sources returns the complete list of URLs." Real-time third-party feeds are labeled as `oai-sports`, `oai-weather`, or `oai-finance`.

## Response Format

### Responses API Output

The output contains two items:

**1. `web_search_call` item** with:
- `id`: Search call identifier
- `status`: e.g., `"completed"`
- `action`: One of:
  - **`search`** — a web search (usually includes `queries`); incurs a tool call cost
  - **`open_page`** — opening a page (reasoning models)
  - **`find_in_page`** — searching within a page (reasoning models)

**2. `message` item** containing:
- `message.content[0].text` — the text response
- `message.content[0].annotations` — array of `url_citation` objects

```json
[
  {
    "type": "web_search_call",
    "id": "ws_67c9fa05...",
    "status": "completed"
  },
  {
    "type": "message",
    "role": "assistant",
    "content": [
      {
        "type": "output_text",
        "text": "On March 6, 2025, several news...",
        "annotations": [
          {
            "type": "url_citation",
            "start_index": 2606,
            "end_index": 2758,
            "url": "https://...",
            "title": "Title..."
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
]
```

### Chat Completions API Output

```json
{
  "message": {
    "role": "assistant",
    "content": "the model response is here...",
    "annotations": [
      {
        "type": "url_citation",
        "url_citation": {
          "end_index": 985,
          "start_index": 764,
          "title": "Page title...",
          "url": "https://..."
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Citation Requirements

"When displaying web results or information contained in web results to end users, inline citations must be made clearly visible and clickable in your user interface."

## Supported Models

### Responses API (`web_search` tool)
Works with models including `gpt-5`, `o4-mini`, `gpt-4.1`, `gpt-4.1-mini`, and reasoning/deep-research variants.

### Chat Completions API (specialized models only)
- `gpt-5-search-api`
- `gpt-4o-search-preview`
- `gpt-4o-mini-search-preview`

## Pricing

Search actions incur a tool call cost — see OpenAI's pricing page under "built-in tools." Rate limits are the same as the tiered rate limits for the underlying model.

## Limitations

- Not supported with `gpt-5` at `minimal` reasoning or with `gpt-4.1-nano`
- Rate limits match the underlying model's tiered limits
- "Web search is limited to a context window size of 128000" (even with `gpt-4.1` and `gpt-4.1-mini`)

## API Availability Summary

| API | Available |
|-----|-----------|
| Responses API | ✅ |
| Chat Completions API | ✅ (specialized models only) |
| Assistants API | ✅ |

## Comprehensive Agent Setup & LLM Analysis Exploration Report

I've completed a thorough exploration of the Google ADK-Go agent implementation in Changelogue. Here are the detailed findings:

---

### 1. **Agent Architecture Overview**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/ARCH.md` (lines 165-188)

The agent layer is implemented using **Google ADK-Go v0.5.0** with multi-agent orchestration:

- **Root Agent** (`release_analyst`): Orchestrates the full analysis workflow
- **Data Sub-Agent**: Queries releases and context sources from the database
- **Search Sub-Agent**: Performs web searches for additional context (provider-specific)

---

### 2. **Agent Tool Registration & Definition**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go`

Three ADK function tools are registered via `functiontool.New()`:

| Tool | Input | Output | Purpose |
|------|-------|--------|---------|
| `get_releases` | `{page, per_page}` | Paginated release list | Fetch recent releases for the project |
| `get_release_detail` | `{release_id}` | Full release with raw data | Inspect changelogs and metadata |
| `list_context_sources` | `{page, per_page}` | Context source list | Discover runbooks, docs, dashboards |

**Key code**:
```go
// tools.go: NewTools creates ADK function tools scoped to a project
func NewTools(store AgentDataStore, projectID string) ([]tool.Tool, error) {
    f := &toolFactory{store: store, projectID: projectID}
    
    getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
        Name:        "get_releases",
        Description: "Fetch recent releases for the current project...",
    }, f.getReleases)
    // ... similar for get_release_detail, list_context_sources
}
```

Tools are **project-scoped** via `toolFactory`, ensuring all queries are filtered to the specific project ID.

---

### 3. **LLM Model & Provider Support**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go`

The orchestrator supports **two LLM providers**:

```go
type LLMConfig struct {
    Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
    Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"
    
    // Gemini
    GoogleAPIKey string
    
    // OpenAI
    OpenAIAPIKey      string
    OpenAIBaseURL     string
    OpenAISearchModel string // search-capable model for web search
}
```

**Provider selection logic**:
- **Gemini** (default): Uses Google's native SDK (`google.golang.org/genai`) with ADK's `gemini.NewModel()` and `geminitool.GoogleSearch`
- **OpenAI**: Uses custom adapter (`internal/agent/openai/`) implementing `model.LLM` interface with Web Search support

---

### 4. **OpenAI Adapter Implementation**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`

The adapter provides **ADK-compatible OpenAI integration**:

- **`openaiModel` struct**: Implements `model.LLM` interface
- **`GenerateContent()`**: Converts ADK `LLMRequest` → OpenAI chat completion → ADK `LLMResponse`
- **Streaming**: Not implemented (always yields single response)
- **Tool conversion**: Transforms ADK `genai.Tool` definitions into OpenAI function schema
- **Web Search**: Detects `GoogleSearch` marker in request and emits `web_search_options` in HTTP body

**Example request conversion**:
```go
// OpenAI endpoint: POST /v1/chat/completions
{
  "model": "gpt-5.2",
  "messages": [...],  // ADK genai.Content → OpenAI chat messages
  "tools": [...],     // ADK function declarations → OpenAI function schema
  "web_search_options": {}  // enabled if search_agent is used
}
```

---

### 5. **Web Search Tool Setup**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go`

The `WebSearch` tool is provider-specific:

```go
// OpenAI: custom WebSearch tool
searchTool = oaimodel.WebSearch{}

// Gemini: native GoogleSearch tool
searchTool = geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
```

Both tools are registered with the search sub-agent:
```go
searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "search_agent",
    Description: "Search the web for additional context...",
    Model:       searchModel,
    Tools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},  // provider-specific
})
```

---

### 6. **Agent Orchestrator Lifecycle**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

The `Orchestrator` manages the complete agent run:

```go
type Orchestrator struct {
    store     OrchestratorStore
    llmConfig LLMConfig
    publicURL string
}
```

**`RunAgent()` workflow** (lines 224-254):
1. Mark run as "running"
2. Execute agent via `executeAgent()` (detailed below)
3. Parse JSON report from LLM output
4. Create `SemanticRelease` with linked `semantic_release_sources`
5. Send project-level notifications
6. Mark run as "completed"

**`executeAgent()` execution** (lines 267-427):
1. Load project config (custom `agent_prompt`)
2. Build dynamic instruction with version substitution (line 85)
3. Create LLM model via `NewLLMModel()` (supports Gemini or OpenAI)
4. Create project-scoped tools via `NewTools(store, projectID)`
5. Build multi-agent hierarchy:
   - Data agent (function tools)
   - Search agent (web search tool)
   - Root agent (agentools wrapping both)
6. Run via ADK `runner.Runner` with in-memory session
7. Capture final text response
8. Parse as JSON `SemanticReport`
9. Fallback: store raw text if parsing fails

---

### 7. **Agent Worker & River Job Integration**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/worker.go`

The `AgentWorker` implements `river.Worker[queue.AgentJobArgs]`:

```go
func (w *AgentWorker) Work(ctx context.Context, job *river.Job[queue.AgentJobArgs]) error {
    // 1. Load agent run
    run, err := w.store.GetAgentRun(ctx, job.Args.AgentRunID)
    
    // 2. Check multi-source waiting (if WaitForAllSources rule enabled)
    if run.Version != "" && rules.WaitForAllSources {
        ready, _ := w.orchestrator.checkAllSourcesReady(ctx, run.ProjectID, run.Version)
        if !ready && job.Attempt < 12 {
            return river.JobSnooze(5 * time.Minute)  // Retry in 5 min
        }
    }
    
    // 3. Execute agent
    return w.orchestrator.RunAgent(ctx, run)
}
```

**Timeout**: 5 minutes per job (accounts for multiple LLM round-trips)

---

### 8. **Job Queuing & Triggering**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/queue/jobs.go`

```go
type AgentJobArgs struct {
    AgentRunID string `json:"agent_run_id"`
    ProjectID  string `json:"project_id"`
    Version    string `json:"version"`
}

func (AgentJobArgs) Kind() string { return "agent_run" }
```

**Two triggering paths**:

1. **API Manual Trigger** (`/api/v1/projects/{projectId}/agent/run`)
   - Files: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/agent.go` (lines 34-57)
   - Calls `store.TriggerAgentRun(projectID, trigger, version)`

2. **Auto-trigger on Release** (via NotifyWorker)
   - File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/worker.go` (lines 80-150)
   - Checks project's `AgentRules` (on_major_release, on_security_patch, version_pattern, wait_for_all_sources)
   - Calls `store.EnqueueAgentRun()` if criteria match

**Transactional Outbox** (file: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/pgstore.go`, lines 892-914):
```go
func (s *PgStore) EnqueueAgentRun(ctx context.Context, projectID, trigger, version string) error {
    tx, _ := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
    // 1. INSERT agent_runs row
    tx.QueryRow(...INSERT INTO agent_runs...)
    // 2. INSERT River job in same transaction
    s.river.InsertTx(ctx, tx, queue.AgentJobArgs{...}, nil)
    // 3. Commit (zero-loss guarantee)
}
```

### 9. **LLM Configuration & Initialization**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go` (lines 66-86)

Agent worker registration in main:
```go
llmProvider := envOr("LLM_PROVIDER", "gemini")
llmConfig := agentpkg.LLMConfig{
    Provider:          llmProvider,
    Model:             envOr("LLM_MODEL", llmModelDefault),
    GoogleAPIKey:      os.Getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    OpenAIAPIKey:      os.Getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"),
    OpenAIBaseURL:     os.Getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL"),
    OpenAISearchModel: envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api"),
}

agentOrchestrator, err := agentpkg.NewOrchestrator(pgStore, llmConfig, os.Getenv("PUBLIC_URL"))
if err != nil {
    slog.Warn("agent orchestrator not available — agent jobs will not be processed", "err", err)
} else {
    agentWorker := agentpkg.NewAgentWorker(agentOrchestrator, pgStore)
    river.AddWorker(workers, agentWorker)
}
```

**Deployment model**: Single binary with conditional agent worker registration based on API key availability.

The agent is instructed to output a **JSON `SemanticReport`** with these fields:

{

```

The instruction includes version substitution: `{{VERSION}}` → actual target version.

### 12. **Semantic Release Model & Persistence**

**File**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/semantic_release.go`

```go
type SemanticReport struct {
    Subject          string   `json:"subject"`
    Urgency          string   `json:"urgency"`
    UrgencyReason    string   `json:"urgency_reason,omitempty"`
    StatusChecks     []string `json:"status_checks"`
    ChangelogSummary string   `json:"changelog_summary"`
    // ... plus legacy fields for backward compat
}

type SemanticRelease struct {
    ID          string          `json:"id"`
    ProjectID   string          `json:"project_id"`
    Version     string          `json:"version"`
    Report      json.RawMessage `json:"report,omitempty"`  // SemanticReport as JSONB
    Status      string          `json:"status"`  // pending, processing, completed, failed
    CompletedAt *time.Time      `json:"completed_at,omitempty"`
}
```

**Persistence** (orchestrator.go, lines 379-413):
- Gathers all releases matching target version
- Creates `semantic_release_sources` join table entries
- Stores full report as JSONB
- Links to agent run via `UpdateAgentRunResult()`

### 13. **Key Data Interfaces & Store Contracts**

**Agent Data Access** (`tools.go`, line 15):
```go
type AgentDataStore interface {
    ListReleasesByProject(ctx, projectID, page, perPage, includeExcluded)
    GetRelease(ctx, id)
    ListContextSources(ctx, projectID, page, perPage)
}
```

**Orchestrator Store** (`orchestrator.go`, lines 33-44):
```go
type OrchestratorStore interface {
    AgentDataStore
    GetProject(ctx, id)
    GetAgentRun(ctx, id)
    UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, id, status)
    CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs)
    UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, id, semanticReleaseID)
    ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, projectID)
    GetChannel(ctx, id)
    ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, page, perPage)
    HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, sourceID, version)
}
```

All implemented by `api.PgStore`.

---

### 14. **Graceful Degradation**

The system gracefully degrades when LLM API keys are unavailable:

1. **Server startup** (main.go, line 79-86):
   - `NewOrchestrator()` returns error if API key missing
   - Agent worker is NOT registered
   - Rest of system (ingestion, notifications) continues

2. **At runtime**:
   - Agent jobs remain in River queue unprocessed
   - Restart server with API key configured to resume

3. **Agent run failure**:
   - Marked as "failed" with error captured
   - JSON parse failure: falls back to storing raw text as summary

     1→package openai

     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
     9→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// WebSearch is a tool that enables OpenAI's built-in web search via
    13→// web_search_options. It mirrors geminitool.GoogleSearch by adding a
    14→// GoogleSearch marker to the request config, which the OpenAI wrapper
    15→// translates into the web_search_options HTTP body field.
    16→type WebSearch struct{}
    17→
    18→// Name implements tool.Tool.
    19→func (WebSearch) Name() string { return "web_search" }
    20→
    21→// Description implements tool.Tool.
    22→func (WebSearch) Description() string {
    23→	return "Search the web for additional context about a release."
    24→}
    25→
    26→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
    27→func (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool { return false }
    28→
    29→// ProcessRequest implements toolinternal.RequestProcessor by adding a
    30→// GoogleSearch marker to the LLM request. The OpenAI model wrapper
    31→// detects this marker and emits web_search_options in the HTTP body.
    32→func (WebSearch) ProcessRequest(_ tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
    33→	if req == nil {
    34→		return fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")
    35→	}
    36→	if req.Config == nil {
    37→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
    38→	}
    39→	req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
    40→		GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},
    41→	})
    42→	return nil
    43→}
    44→

     1→package openai
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"io"
     9→	"iter"
    10→	"net/http"
    11→	"strings"
    12→
    13→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    14→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    15→)
    16→
    17→const defaultBaseURL = "https://api.openai.com/v1"
    18→
    19→// Config holds connection parameters for an OpenAI-compatible API.
    20→type Config struct {
    21→	APIKey  string
    22→	BaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    23→}
    24→
    25→// openaiModel implements model.LLM for OpenAI-compatible chat completion APIs.
    26→type openaiModel struct {
    27→	name    string
    28→	apiKey  string
    29→	baseURL string
    30→	client  *http.Client
    31→}
    32→
    33→// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.
    34→func NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config) (model.LLM, error) {
    35→	if cfg.APIKey == "" {
    36→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API key is required")
    37→	}
    38→	baseURL := cfg.BaseURL
    39→	if baseURL == "" {
    40→		baseURL = defaultBaseURL
    41→	}
    42→	return &openaiModel{
    43→		name:    modelName,
    44→		apiKey:  cfg.APIKey,
    45→		baseURL: baseURL,
    46→		client:  &http.Client{},
    47→	}, nil
    48→}
    49→
    50→func (m *openaiModel) Name() string { return m.name }
    51→
    52→// GenerateContent converts the ADK request to an OpenAI chat completion request,
    53→// calls the API, and converts the response back to an LLMResponse.
    54→// Streaming is not implemented; the iterator always yields a single response.
    55→func (m *openaiModel) GenerateContent(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest, _ bool) iter.Seq2[*model.LLMResponse, error] {
    56→	return func(yield func(*model.LLMResponse, error) bool) {
    57→		resp, err := m.doRequest(ctx, req)
    58→		if err != nil {
    59→			yield(nil, err)
    60→			return
    61→		}
    62→		yield(resp, nil)
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// Close is a no-op; the HTTP client has no persistent state.
    67→func (m *openaiModel) Close() {}
    68→
    69→// --- OpenAI API types ---
    70→
    71→type webSearchOptions struct{}
    72→
    73→type chatRequest struct {
    74→	Model            string            `json:"model"`
    75→	Messages         []chatMessage     `json:"messages"`
    76→	Tools            []chatTool        `json:"tools,omitempty"`
    77→	Temperature      *float32          `json:"temperature,omitempty"`
    78→	TopP             *float32          `json:"top_p,omitempty"`
    79→	MaxTokens        int32             `json:"max_tokens,omitempty"`
    80→	Stop             []string          `json:"stop,omitempty"`
    81→	Stream           bool              `json:"stream"`
    82→	WebSearchOptions *webSearchOptions `json:"web_search_options,omitempty"`
    83→}
    84→
    85→type chatMessage struct {
    86→	Role       string     `json:"role"`
    87→	Content    string     `json:"content,omitempty"`
    88→	ToolCalls  []toolCall `json:"tool_calls,omitempty"`
    89→	ToolCallID string     `json:"tool_call_id,omitempty"`
    90→}
    91→
    92→type toolCall struct {
    93→	ID       string       `json:"id"`
    94→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
    95→	Function functionCall `json:"function"`
    96→}
    97→
    98→type functionCall struct {
    99→	Name      string `json:"name"`
   100→	Arguments string `json:"arguments"`
   101→}
   102→
   103→type chatTool struct {
   104→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
   105→	Function chatFunction `json:"function"`
   106→}
   107→
   108→type chatFunction struct {
   109→	Name        string `json:"name"`
   110→	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
   111→	Parameters  any    `json:"parameters,omitempty"`
   112→}
   113→
   114→type chatResponse struct {
   115→	Choices []chatChoice `json:"choices"`
   116→	Usage   *chatUsage   `json:"usage,omitempty"`
   117→	Error   *apiError    `json:"error,omitempty"`
   118→}
   119→
   120→type chatChoice struct {
   121→	Message      chatMessage `json:"message"`
   122→	FinishReason string      `json:"finish_reason"`
   123→}
   124→
   125→type chatUsage struct {
   126→	PromptTokens     int32 `json:"prompt_tokens"`
   127→	CompletionTokens int32 `json:"completion_tokens"`
   128→	TotalTokens      int32 `json:"total_tokens"`
   129→}
   130→
   131→type apiError struct {
   132→	Message string `json:"message"`
   133→	Type    string `json:"type"`
   134→	Code    string `json:"code"`
   135→}
   136→
   137→// --- request/response conversion ---
   138→
   139→func (m *openaiModel) doRequest(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   140→	msgs := convertContents(req)
   141→	tools := convertTools(req)
   142→
   143→	chatReq := chatRequest{
   144→		Model:    m.name,
   145→		Messages: msgs,
   146→		Tools:    tools,
   147→		Stream:   false,
   148→	}
   149→
   150→	if req.Config != nil {
   151→		chatReq.Temperature = req.Config.Temperature
   152→		chatReq.TopP = req.Config.TopP
   153→		if req.Config.MaxOutputTokens > 0 {
   154→			chatReq.MaxTokens = req.Config.MaxOutputTokens
   155→		}
   156→		if len(req.Config.StopSequences) > 0 {
   157→			chatReq.Stop = req.Config.StopSequences
   158→		}
   159→	}
   160→
   161→	if hasWebSearch(req) {
   162→		chatReq.WebSearchOptions = &webSearchOptions{}
   163→	}
   164→
   165→	body, err := json.Marshal(chatReq)
   166→	if err != nil {
   167→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: marshal request: %w", err)
   168→	}
   169→
   170→	httpReq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, m.baseURL+"/chat/completions", bytes.NewReader(body))
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: create http request: %w", err)
   173→	}
   174→	httpReq.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   175→	httpReq.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+m.apiKey)
   176→
   177→	httpResp, err := m.client.Do(httpReq)
   178→	if err != nil {
   179→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: http request: %w", err)
   180→	}
   181→	defer httpResp.Body.Close()
   182→
   183→	respBody, err := io.ReadAll(httpResp.Body)
   184→	if err != nil {
   185→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: read response: %w", err)
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	if httpResp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
   189→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API returned status %d: %s", httpResp.StatusCode, string(respBody))
   190→	}
   191→
   192→	var chatResp chatResponse
   193→	if err := json.Unmarshal(respBody, &chatResp); err != nil {
   194→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: unmarshal response: %w", err)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	if chatResp.Error != nil {
   198→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API error: %s (type=%s, code=%s)",
   199→			chatResp.Error.Message, chatResp.Error.Type, chatResp.Error.Code)
   200→	}
   201→
   202→	return convertResponse(&chatResp), nil
   203→}
   204→
   205→// convertContents transforms ADK genai.Content messages into OpenAI chat messages.
   206→func convertContents(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatMessage {
   207→	var msgs []chatMessage
   208→
   209→	// System instruction.
   210→	if req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil {
   211→		var text string
   212→		for _, p := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {
   213→			if p.Text != "" {
   214→				text += p.Text
   215→			}
   216→		}
   217→		if text != "" {
   218→			msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: "system", Content: text})
   219→		}
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	// Conversation contents.
   223→	for _, c := range req.Contents {
   224→		role := mapRole(c.Role)
   225→
   226→		// Check each part: could be text, function_call, or function_response.
   227→		for _, p := range c.Parts {
   228→			switch {
   229→			case p.FunctionCall != nil:
   230→				argsJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionCall.Args)
   231→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   232→					Role: "assistant",
   233→					ToolCalls: []toolCall{{
   234→						ID:   p.FunctionCall.ID,
   235→						Type: "function",
   236→						Function: functionCall{
   237→							Name:      p.FunctionCall.Name,
   238→							Arguments: string(argsJSON),
   239→						},
   240→					}},
   241→				})
   242→			case p.FunctionResponse != nil:
   243→				respJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionResponse.Response)
   244→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   245→					Role:       "tool",
   246→					Content:    string(respJSON),
   247→					ToolCallID: p.FunctionResponse.ID,
   248→				})
   249→			case p.Text != "":
   250→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: role, Content: p.Text})
   251→			}
   252→		}
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	// Merge consecutive assistant messages with tool_calls.
   256→	msgs = mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs)
   257→
   258→	return msgs
   259→}
   260→
   261→// mergeAssistantToolCalls combines adjacent assistant messages that each have
   262→// tool_calls into a single message, as OpenAI expects multiple tool_calls in
   263→// one message rather than separate messages.
   264→func mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs []chatMessage) []chatMessage {
   265→	if len(msgs) == 0 {
   266→		return msgs
   267→	}
   268→	var merged []chatMessage
   269→	for _, msg := range msgs {
   270→		if msg.Role == "assistant" && len(msg.ToolCalls) > 0 && len(merged) > 0 {
   271→			prev := &merged[len(merged)-1]
   272→			if prev.Role == "assistant" && len(prev.ToolCalls) > 0 {
   273→				prev.ToolCalls = append(prev.ToolCalls, msg.ToolCalls...)
   274→				continue
   275→			}
   276→		}
   277→		merged = append(merged, msg)
   278→	}
   279→	return merged
   280→}
   281→
   282→// hasWebSearch checks whether the request contains a GoogleSearch tool marker,
   283→// indicating the caller wants web search capability.
   284→func hasWebSearch(req *model.LLMRequest) bool {
   285→	if req.Config == nil {
   286→		return false
   287→	}
   288→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   289→		if t != nil && t.GoogleSearch != nil {
   290→			return true
   291→		}
   292→	}
   293→	return false
   294→}
   295→
   296→// convertTools transforms ADK genai.Tool definitions into OpenAI tool format.
   297→func convertTools(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatTool {
   298→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   299→		return nil
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	var tools []chatTool
   303→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   304→		if t.GoogleSearch != nil {
   305→			continue
   306→		}
   307→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   308→			ct := chatTool{
   309→				Type: "function",
   310→				Function: chatFunction{
   311→					Name:        fd.Name,
   312→					Description: fd.Description,
   313→				},
   314→			}
   315→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   316→				ct.Function.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   317→			}
   318→			tools = append(tools, ct)
   319→		}
   320→	}
   321→	return tools
   322→}
   323→
   324→// schemaToMap converts a genai.Schema to a JSON-serialisable map that matches
   325→// the OpenAI function parameters format (JSON Schema).
   326→func schemaToMap(s *genai.Schema) map[string]any {
   327→	m := map[string]any{}
   328→	if s.Type != "" {
   329→		m["type"] = strings.ToLower(string(s.Type))
   330→	}
   331→	if s.Description != "" {
   332→		m["description"] = s.Description
   333→	}
   334→	if len(s.Enum) > 0 {
   335→		m["enum"] = s.Enum
   336→	}
   337→	if s.Items != nil {
   338→		m["items"] = schemaToMap(s.Items)
   339→	}
   340→	if len(s.Properties) > 0 {
   341→		props := map[string]any{}
   342→		for k, v := range s.Properties {
   343→			props[k] = schemaToMap(v)
   344→		}
   345→		m["properties"] = props
   346→	}
   347→	if len(s.Required) > 0 {
   348→		m["required"] = s.Required
   349→	}
   350→	return m
   351→}
   352→
   353→// convertResponse transforms an OpenAI chat completion response into an ADK LLMResponse.
   354→func convertResponse(resp *chatResponse) *model.LLMResponse {
   355→	llmResp := &model.LLMResponse{
   356→		TurnComplete: true,
   357→	}
   358→
   359→	if len(resp.Choices) == 0 {
   360→		llmResp.Content = genai.NewContentFromText("", "model")
   361→		return llmResp
   362→	}
   363→
   364→	choice := resp.Choices[0]
   365→	llmResp.FinishReason = mapFinishReason(choice.FinishReason)
   366→
   367→	content := &genai.Content{Role: "model"}
   368→
   369→	// Text content.
   370→	if choice.Message.Content != "" {
   371→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: choice.Message.Content})
   372→	}
   373→
   374→	// Tool calls.
   375→	for _, tc := range choice.Message.ToolCalls {
   376→		var args map[string]any
   377→		if tc.Function.Arguments != "" {
   378→			_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(tc.Function.Arguments), &args)
   379→		}
   380→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{
   381→			FunctionCall: &genai.FunctionCall{
   382→				ID:   tc.ID,
   383→				Name: tc.Function.Name,
   384→				Args: args,
   385→			},
   386→		})
   387→	}
   388→
   389→	llmResp.Content = content
   390→
   391→	if resp.Usage != nil {
   392→		llmResp.UsageMetadata = &genai.GenerateContentResponseUsageMetadata{
   393→			PromptTokenCount:     resp.Usage.PromptTokens,
   394→			CandidatesTokenCount: resp.Usage.CompletionTokens,
   395→			TotalTokenCount:      resp.Usage.TotalTokens,
   396→		}
   397→	}
   398→
   399→	return llmResp
   400→}
   401→
   402→func mapRole(role string) string {
   403→	switch role {
   404→	case "model":
   405→		return "assistant"
   406→	default:
   407→		return role
   408→	}
   409→}
   410→
   411→func mapFinishReason(reason string) genai.FinishReason {
   412→	switch reason {
   413→	case "stop":
   414→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   415→	case "length":
   416→		return genai.FinishReasonMaxTokens
   417→	case "tool_calls":
   418→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   419→	case "content_filter":
   420→		return genai.FinishReasonSafety
   421→	default:
   422→		return genai.FinishReasonOther
   423→	}
   424→}
   425→

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini"
     9→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    10→
    11→	oaimodel "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent/openai"
    12→)
    13→
    14→// LLMConfig holds the configuration for creating an LLM model.
    15→type LLMConfig struct {
    16→	Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
    17→	Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"
    18→
    19→	// Gemini
    20→	GoogleAPIKey string
    21→
    22→	// OpenAI
    23→	OpenAIAPIKey      string
    24→	OpenAIBaseURL     string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    25→	OpenAISearchModel string // search-capable model for web search sub-agent
    26→}
    27→
    28→// NewLLMModel creates a model.LLM based on the configured provider.
    29→func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig) (model.LLM, error) {
    30→	switch cfg.Provider {
    31→	case "gemini", "":
    32→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
    33→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is required for gemini provider")
    34→		}
    35→		return gemini.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, &genai.ClientConfig{
    36→			APIKey: cfg.GoogleAPIKey,
    37→		})
    38→
    39→	case "openai":
    40→		return oaimodel.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, oaimodel.Config{
    41→			APIKey:  cfg.OpenAIAPIKey,
    42→			BaseURL: cfg.OpenAIBaseURL,
    43→		})
    44→
    45→	default:
    46→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown LLM provider: %q (supported: gemini, openai)", cfg.Provider)
    47→	}
    48→}
    49→

    19→	"google.golang.org/adk/runner"
    20→	"google.golang.org/adk/session"
    21→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    22→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool/agenttool"
    23→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool"
    24→
    25→	oaimodel "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent/openai"
    26→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"
    27→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/routing"
    28→)
    29→
    30→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    31→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    32→// the resulting semantic release.
    33→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    34→	AgentDataStore
    35→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    36→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    37→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    38→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    39→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    40→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    41→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    42→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    43→	HasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)
    44→}
    45→
    46→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    47→
    48→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    49→
    50→Use the available tools to:
    51→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    52→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    53→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    54→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    55→5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
    56→   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).
    57→
    58→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    59→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    60→
    61→For download_links: prefer direct binary/artifact URLs for specific platforms (e.g., linux-amd64, darwin-arm64, windows-amd64 .tar.gz/.zip files) over generic release pages. Include the general release page URL as well, but prioritize direct download links when available.
    62→
    63→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    64→{
    65→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Urgency Summary>)",
    66→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    67→  "urgency_reason": "Why this urgency level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    68→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    69→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    70→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    71→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    72→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    73→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    74→  "download_links": ["https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-linux-amd64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-darwin-arm64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    75→}`
    76→
    77→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    78→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    79→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.
    80→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
    81→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
    82→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
    83→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
    84→	}
    85→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
    86→
    87→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
    88→	if err != nil {
    89→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
    90→	}
    91→
    92→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
    93→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
    94→	if err != nil {
    95→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
    99→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   100→		Name:        "data_agent",
   101→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   102→		Model:       llmModel,
   103→		Tools:       functionTools,
   104→	})
   105→	if err != nil {
   106→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   107→	}
   108→
   109→	// Search sub-agent: provider-specific web search.
   110→	var searchTool tool.Tool
   111→	var searchModel model.LLM
   112→	switch llmConfig.Provider {
   113→	case "openai":
   114→		searchTool = oaimodel.WebSearch{}
   115→		// OpenAI web search requires a search-capable model.
   116→		searchModelName := llmConfig.OpenAISearchModel
   117→		if searchModelName == "" {
   118→			searchModelName = "gpt-5-search-api"
   119→		}
   120→		searchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, LLMConfig{
   121→			Provider:      "openai",
   122→			Model:         searchModelName,
   123→			OpenAIAPIKey:  llmConfig.OpenAIAPIKey,
   124→			OpenAIBaseURL: llmConfig.OpenAIBaseURL,
   125→		})
   126→		if err != nil {
   127→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create OpenAI search model: %w", err)
   128→		}
   129→	default: // gemini
   130→		searchTool = geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
   131→		searchModel = llmModel
   132→	}
   133→
   134→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   135→		Name:        "search_agent",
   136→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   137→		Model:       searchModel,
   138→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},
   139→	})
   140→	if err != nil {
   141→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   145→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   146→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   147→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   148→		Model:       llmModel,
   149→		Instruction: instruction,
   150→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   151→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   152→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   153→		},
   154→	})
   155→}
   156→
   157→// checkAllSourcesReady returns true if every source in the project has a
   158→// release matching the target version.
   159→func (o *Orchestrator) checkAllSourcesReady(ctx context.Context, projectID, version string) (bool, error) {
   160→	// Use a high limit to fetch all sources; projects rarely exceed a handful.
   161→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, 1, 1000)
   162→	if err != nil {
   163→		return false, fmt.Errorf("list sources: %w", err)
   164→	}
   165→	if len(sources) == 0 {
   166→		return true, nil
   167→	}
   168→
   169→	for _, src := range sources {
   170→		has, err := o.store.HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, src.ID, version)
   171→		if err != nil {
   172→			return false, fmt.Errorf("check source %s: %w", src.ID, err)
   173→		}
   174→		if !has {
   175→			slog.Info("agent: source not ready for version",
   176→				"project_id", projectID,
   177→				"source_id", src.ID,
   178→				"version", version,
   179→			)
   180→			return false, nil
   181→		}
   182→	}
   183→	return true, nil
   184→}
   185→
   186→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
   187→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
   188→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
   189→type Orchestrator struct {
   190→	store     OrchestratorStore
   191→	llmConfig LLMConfig
   192→	publicURL string // base URL for internal Changelogue links (e.g. "https://changelogue.example.com")
   193→}
   194→
   195→// NewOrchestrator creates a new Orchestrator. It validates that the provided
   196→// LLMConfig has the required API key for the configured provider. If the key
   197→// is missing, it returns an error so the caller can degrade gracefully.
   198→func NewOrchestrator(store OrchestratorStore, cfg LLMConfig, publicURL string) (*Orchestrator, error) {
   199→	switch cfg.Provider {
   200→	case "openai":
   201→		if cfg.OpenAIAPIKey == "" {
   202→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   203→		}
   204→	default: // gemini
   205→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
   206→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   207→		}
   208→	}
   209→	return &Orchestrator{
   210→		store:     store,
   211→		llmConfig: cfg,
   212→		publicURL: strings.TrimRight(publicURL, "/"),
   213→	}, nil
   214→}
   215→
   216→// RunAgent executes the full agent lifecycle for a given agent run:
   217→//  1. Marks the run as "running".
   218→//  2. Loads the project and builds the agent prompt.
   219→//  3. Creates an ADK-Go LLM agent with project-scoped tools.
   220→//  4. Runs the agent and captures its final text response.
   221→//  5. Parses the response into a SemanticReport.
   222→//  6. Creates a SemanticRelease and links it to the agent run.
   223→//  7. Marks the run as "completed" (or "failed" on error).
   224→func (o *Orchestrator) RunAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) error {
   225→	// Mark as running.
   226→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "running"); err != nil {
   227→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to running: %w", err)
   228→	}
   229→
   230→	// Execute the agent; capture any error to mark the run as failed.
   231→	result, err := o.executeAgent(ctx, run)
   232→	if err != nil {
   233→		slog.Error("agent run failed", "run_id", run.ID, "err", err)
   234→		// Best-effort: mark the run as failed.
   235→		if statusErr := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "failed"); statusErr != nil {
   236→			slog.Error("agent: failed to mark run as failed",
   237→				"run_id", run.ID, "status_err", statusErr)
   238→		}
   239→		return err
   240→	}
   241→
   242→	// Link the semantic release to the agent run and mark completed.
   243→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, run.ID, result.semanticReleaseID); err != nil {
   244→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run result: %w", err)
   245→	}
   246→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "completed"); err != nil {
   247→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to completed: %w", err)
   248→	}
   249→
   250→	// Send project-level notifications (best-effort; errors are logged, not returned).
   251→	o.sendProjectNotifications(ctx, run, result)
   252→
   253→	return nil
   254→}
   255→
   256→// agentResult holds the output of a successful agent execution, used to
   257→// construct notifications after the semantic release is persisted.
   258→type agentResult struct {
   259→	semanticReleaseID string
   260→	version           string
   261→	reportText        string
   262→	projectName       string
   263→}
   264→
   265→// executeAgent performs the actual LLM agent interaction and returns the
   266→// agentResult containing the semantic release ID and metadata for notifications.
   267→func (o *Orchestrator) executeAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) (*agentResult, error) {
   268→	// Load project.
   269→	slog.Info("agent: loading project", "run_id", run.ID, "project_id", run.ProjectID)
   270→	project, err := o.store.GetProject(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   271→	if err != nil {
   272→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get project: %w", err)
   273→	}
   274→	slog.Info("agent: project loaded", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   275→
   276→	// Extract target version from the agent run.
   277→	version := run.Version
   278→	if version == "" {
   279→		// Fallback: parse from trigger "auto:version:v1.10.15"
   280→		if strings.HasPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:") {
   281→			version = strings.TrimPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:")
   282→		}
   283→	}
   284→
   285→	// Build the agent using the shared constructor.
   286→	slog.Info("agent: building agent", "run_id", run.ID,
   287→		"provider", o.llmConfig.Provider, "model", o.llmConfig.Model,
   288→		"version", version)
   289→	agentInstance, err := BuildAgent(ctx, o.store, project, o.llmConfig, version)
   290→	if err != nil {
   291→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("build agent: %w", err)
   292→	}
   293→
   294→	// Create in-memory session service and a new session.
   295→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   296→	createResp, err := sessionService.Create(ctx, &session.CreateRequest{
   297→		AppName: "changelogue",
   298→		UserID:  "system",
   299→	})
   300→	if err != nil {
   301→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create session: %w", err)
   302→	}
   303→	sess := createResp.Session
   304→
   305→	// Create the runner with logging plugin for agent trace visibility.
   306→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   307→		AppName:        "changelogue",
   308→		Agent:          agentInstance,
   309→		SessionService: sessionService,
   310→		PluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{
   311→			Plugins:      []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")},
   312→			CloseTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
   313→		},
   314→	})
   315→	if err != nil {
   316→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create runner: %w", err)
   317→	}
   318→
   319→	// Run the agent with a prompt requesting analysis.
   320→	slog.Info("agent: starting LLM run", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name, "version", version)
   321→	var userPrompt string
   322→	if version != "" {
   323→		userPrompt = fmt.Sprintf("Analyze version %s for this project. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report.", version)
   324→	} else {
   325→		userPrompt = "Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report."
   326→	}
   327→	userMsg := genai.NewContentFromText(userPrompt, "user")
   328→
   329→	var finalText string
   330→	eventCount := 0
   331→	for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
   332→		if err != nil {
   333→			slog.Error("agent: run event error",
   334→				"run_id", run.ID,
   335→				"event_count", eventCount,
   336→				"err", err,
   337→			)
   338→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
   339→		}
   340→		eventCount++
   341→		if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
   342→			for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   343→				if part.Text != "" {
   344→					finalText += part.Text
   345→				}
   346→			}
   347→		}
   348→	}
   349→	slog.Info("agent: LLM run finished",
   350→		"run_id", run.ID,
   351→		"event_count", eventCount,
   352→		"output_length", len(finalText),
   353→	)
   354→
   355→	if finalText == "" {
   356→		slog.Error("agent: produced no output",
   357→			"run_id", run.ID,
   358→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
   359→			"event_count", eventCount,
   360→		)
   361→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent produced no output")
   362→	}
   363→
   364→	// Parse the agent's response into a SemanticReport.
   365→	report, err := parseReport(finalText)
   366→	if err != nil {
   367→		slog.Warn("agent output was not valid JSON report, storing raw",
   368→			"run_id", run.ID, "parse_err", err, "raw_output", finalText)
   369→		// Fall back to storing the raw text as the summary.
   370→		report = &models.SemanticReport{
   371→			Summary: finalText,
   372→		}
   373→	}
   374→
   375→	reportJSON, err := json.Marshal(report)
   376→	if err != nil {
   377→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("marshal report: %w", err)
   378→	}
   379→
   380→	// Gather release IDs for the semantic_release_sources join table.
   381→	// Only link releases that match the target version.
   382→	releases, _, err := o.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 200, false)
   383→	if err != nil {
   384→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list releases for semantic release: %w", err)
   385→	}
   386→	releaseIDs := make([]string, 0, len(releases))
   387→	for _, r := range releases {
   388→		if r.Version == version {
   389→			releaseIDs = append(releaseIDs, r.ID)
   390→		}
   391→	}
   392→
   393→	// Use the target version for the semantic release.
   394→	srVersion := version
   395→	if srVersion == "" {
   396→		srVersion = "unknown"
   397→		if len(releases) > 0 {
   398→			srVersion = releases[0].Version
   399→		}
   400→	}
   401→
   402→	now := time.Now()
   403→	sr := &models.SemanticRelease{
   404→		ProjectID:   run.ProjectID,
   405→		Version:     srVersion,
   406→		Report:      reportJSON,
   407→		Status:      "completed",
   408→		CompletedAt: &now,
   409→	}
   410→
   411→	if err := o.store.CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs); err != nil {
   412→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create semantic release: %w", err)
   413→	}
   414→
   415→	slog.Info("agent run produced semantic release",
   416→		"run_id", run.ID,
   417→		"semantic_release_id", sr.ID,
   418→		"version", sr.Version,
   419→	)
   420→
   421→	return &agentResult{
   422→		semanticReleaseID: sr.ID,
   423→		version:           srVersion,
   424→		reportText:        finalText,
   425→		projectName:       project.Name,
   426→	}, nil
   427→}
   428→
   429→// defaultSenders returns a map of channel type to Sender for the supported
   430→// notification channel types (webhook, slack, discord).
   431→func defaultSenders() map[string]routing.Sender {
   432→	httpClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}
   433→	return map[string]routing.Sender{
   434→		"webhook": &routing.WebhookSender{Client: httpClient},
   435→		"slack":   &routing.SlackSender{Client: httpClient},
   436→		"discord": &routing.DiscordSender{Client: httpClient},
   437→	}
   438→}
   439→
   440→// sendProjectNotifications looks up project-level subscriptions and sends a
   441→// notification to each channel with the semantic release report. This is
   442→// best-effort: errors are logged but do not propagate to the caller.
   443→func (o *Orchestrator) sendProjectNotifications(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun, result *agentResult) {
   444→	subs, err := o.store.ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   445→	if err != nil {
   446→		slog.Error("list project subscriptions for notification",
   447→			"project_id", run.ProjectID, "err", err)
   448→		return
   449→	}
   450→	if len(subs) == 0 {
   451→		slog.Debug("agent: no project subscriptions configured",
   452→			"project_id", run.ProjectID)
   453→		return
   454→	}
   455→
   456→	senders := defaultSenders()
   457→
   458→	msg := routing.Notification{
   459→		Title:       fmt.Sprintf("Semantic Release Report: %s %s", result.projectName, result.version),
   460→		Body:        result.reportText,
   461→		Version:     result.version,
   462→		ProjectName: result.projectName,
   463→	}
   464→
   465→	// Build ReleaseURL for the semantic release detail page.
   466→	if o.publicURL != "" {
   467→		msg.ReleaseURL = fmt.Sprintf("%s/projects/%s/semantic-releases/%s", o.publicURL, run.ProjectID, result.semanticReleaseID)
   468→	}
   469→
   470→	// Look up the first source for provider/repo info to build SourceURL.
   471→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 1)
   472→	if err == nil && len(sources) > 0 {
   473→		msg.Provider = sources[0].Provider
   474→		msg.Repository = sources[0].Repository
   475→		msg.SourceURL = routing.ProviderURL(sources[0].Provider, sources[0].Repository, result.version)
   476→	}
   477→
   478→	for _, sub := range subs {
   479→		ch, err := o.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
   480→		if err != nil {
   481→			slog.Error("get channel for project notification",
   482→				"channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
   483→			continue
   484→		}
   485→
   486→		sender, ok := senders[ch.Type]
   487→		if !ok {
   488→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type for project notification", "type", ch.Type)
   489→			continue
   490→		}
   491→
   492→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
   493→			slog.Error("send project notification failed",
   494→				"channel", ch.Name, "type", ch.Type, "err", err)
   495→		} else {
   496→			slog.Info("project notification sent",
   497→				"channel", ch.Name, "project_id", run.ProjectID, "version", result.version)
   498→		}
   499→	}
   500→}
   501→
   502→// parseReport attempts to parse the agent's text output as a SemanticReport JSON.
   503→// It handles cases where the agent wraps the JSON in markdown code blocks.
   504→func parseReport(text string) (*models.SemanticReport, error) {
   505→	// Strip markdown code fences if present.
   506→	cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(text)
   507→	if strings.HasPrefix(cleaned, "```") {
   508→		// Remove opening fence (with optional language tag).
   509→		if idx := strings.Index(cleaned, "\n"); idx != -1 {
   510→			cleaned = cleaned[idx+1:]
   511→		}
   512→		// Remove closing fence.
   513→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(cleaned, "```"); idx != -1 {
   514→			cleaned = cleaned[:idx]
   515→		}
   516→		cleaned = strings.TrimSpace(cleaned)
   517→	}
   518→
   519→	var report models.SemanticReport
   520→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(cleaned), &report); err != nil {
   521→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse report JSON: %w", err)
   522→	}
   523→
   524→	if report.Subject == "" && report.Summary == "" {
   525→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("report is missing required 'subject' or 'summary' field")
   526→	}
   527→
   528→	return &report, nil
   529→}
   530→

> AGENT

Now I have a good picture of the current state. Let me ask a clarifying question.

I see that you already have an OpenAI web search integration, but it uses the **Chat Completions API** with `web_search_options: {}` and a dedicated search model (`gpt-5-search-api`). This approach has a key limitation: the search is implicit — the model decides when/what to search, but you can't control parameters like `search_context_size`, `user_location`, or `allowed_domains`.

The OpenAI docs show a newer **Responses API** approach with `tools: [{type: "web_search"}]` that gives more control and works with standard models (gpt-5, gpt-4.1, etc.) rather than requiring special `-search-` model variants.

Before I propose approaches, let me understand your intent better:

> AGENT

Good. So the goal is to migrate the OpenAI adapter from the **Chat Completions API** to the **Responses API**, gaining access to the proper `web_search` tool with parameters like domain filtering, search context size, and user location.

Here are the approaches I see:

## Approach 1: Full Responses API migration (Recommended)

Replace the entire `internal/agent/openai/openai.go` adapter to call `/v1/responses` instead of `/v1/chat/completions`. This means:

- New request/response types matching the Responses API format (`input`, `tools: [{type: "web_search", ...}]`, output items)
- The `WebSearch` tool can now carry config (allowed_domains, search_context_size)
- No more need for a separate `OpenAISearchModel` — the same model handles search natively
- **Trade-off**: Larger change, touches the core adapter. The Responses API uses a different message format (`input` items vs `messages` array). But it's a clean break.

## Approach 2: Hybrid — add Responses API alongside Chat Completions

Keep the existing Chat Completions adapter for the main model, but add a second adapter that uses the Responses API specifically for web search calls. The search sub-agent would use the Responses API adapter.

- **Trade-off**: Two code paths to maintain, but lower risk since the main agent path is unchanged.

## Approach 3: Chat Completions with enhanced web_search_options

Stay on Chat Completions but enhance `web_search_options` with the parameters it does support (it supports `search_context_size` and `user_location` even on Chat Completions).

- **Trade-off**: Minimal change, but Chat Completions web search only works with special `-search-` models and has fewer features than the Responses API. Doesn't give you domain filtering.

**My recommendation is Approach 1** — a full migration to the Responses API. The Responses API is OpenAI's primary path forward, supports all models (no special search model needed), and gives full control over web search parameters. The Chat Completions search models are legacy. This also simplifies `LLMConfig` by removing `OpenAISearchModel`.

> AGENT

## Design: Migrate OpenAI Adapter to Responses API

### Architecture

The core change is replacing `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `POST /v1/responses`. This changes the wire format but the ADK interface (`model.LLM`) stays the same.

**Request format changes:**
- `messages` array → `input` (string or array of input items)
- `tools: [{type: "function", function: {...}}]` → `tools: [{type: "function", ...}, {type: "web_search", ...}]`
- `web_search_options: {}` (separate field) → `tools: [{type: "web_search", search_context_size: "medium", user_location: {...}}]` (native tool)

**Response format changes:**
- `choices[0].message.content` → `output` array with `message` items
- `choices[0].message.tool_calls` → `output` items with `type: "function_call"`
- Annotations (citations) come embedded in `output_text.annotations`

### Key Components

1. **`openai.go`** — Rewrite `doRequest()` and all type definitions to use `/v1/responses` format
2. **`websearch.go`** — `WebSearch` tool now carries optional config (allowed_domains, search_context_size) that gets encoded directly into the `tools` array
3. **`model.go`** — Remove `OpenAISearchModel` from `LLMConfig` since any model can now do web search
4. **`orchestrator.go`** — Simplify search sub-agent creation (no separate search model needed)
5. **`cmd/server/main.go`** — Remove `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` env var

### WebSearch Tool Config

```go
type WebSearch struct {
    SearchContextSize string   // "low", "medium" (default), "high"
    AllowedDomains    []string // optional domain filter
}
```

### Simplification

The search sub-agent no longer needs a separate model. When `provider=openai`, both the data agent and search agent can use the same model — web search is just another tool in the `tools` array. This eliminates the `OpenAISearchModel` config and the second `NewLLMModel()` call in `BuildAgent`.

Does this design look right so far?

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/plans/2026-03-04-openai-responses-api-design.md",
  "content": "# Design: Migrate OpenAI Adapter to Responses API\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-04\n**Status:** Approved\n\n## Problem\n\nThe current OpenAI adapter uses the Chat Completions API (`/v1/chat/completions`) with `web_search_options: {}` for web search. This has limitations:\n\n- Requires a dedicated search-capable model (`gpt-5-search-api`) — separate from the main model\n- No control over search parameters (domain filtering, search context size, user location)\n- Chat Completions search models are being superseded by the Responses API\n\n## Solution\n\nMigrate the OpenAI adapter from Chat Completions to the Responses API (`/v1/responses`), which:\n\n- Supports `web_search` as a native tool alongside function tools\n- Works with any supported model (gpt-5, gpt-4.1, o4-mini, etc.)\n- Provides domain filtering (`allowed_domains`), `search_context_size`, and `user_location`\n- Eliminates the need for a separate search model\n\n## Scope\n\n### Files Changed\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `internal/agent/openai/openai.go` | Rewrite request/response types and `doRequest()` for Responses API |\n| `internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go` | Update tests for new wire format |\n| `internal/agent/openai/websearch.go` | Add configurable fields (SearchContextSize, AllowedDomains) |\n| `internal/agent/model.go` | Remove `OpenAISearchModel` from `LLMConfig` |\n| `internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | Simplify search sub-agent (same model, no separate model creation) |\n| `cmd/server/main.go` | Remove `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` env var |\n\n### Request Format (Before → After)\n\n**Before (Chat Completions):**\n```json\n{\n  \"model\": \"gpt-5.2\",\n  \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"...\"}],\n  \"tools\": [{\"type\": \"function\", \"function\": {\"name\": \"get_releases\", ...}}],\n  \"web_search_options\": {}\n}\n```\n\n**After (Responses):**\n```json\n{\n  \"model\": \"gpt-5.2\",\n  \"input\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"...\"}],\n  \"tools\": [\n    {\"type\": \"function\", \"name\": \"get_releases\", \"parameters\": {...}},\n    {\"type\": \"web_search\", \"search_context_size\": \"medium\"}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n### Response Format (Before → After)\n\n**Before:** `choices[0].message.content` + `choices[0].message.tool_calls`\n\n**After:** `output` array containing:\n- `{type: \"web_search_call\", status: \"completed\"}` — search invocation record\n- `{type: \"function_call\", id, name, arguments}` — function tool calls\n- `{type: \"message\", content: [{type: \"output_text\", text, annotations}]}` — final text with citations\n\n### WebSearch Tool Enhancement\n\n```go\ntype WebSearch struct {\n    SearchContextSize string   // \"low\", \"medium\" (default), \"high\"\n    AllowedDomains    []string // optional domain filter\n}\n```\n\nThe `ProcessRequest` method will encode these fields into the tool config rather than using the GoogleSearch marker hack.\n\n### Config Simplification\n\n**Removed from LLMConfig:**\n- `OpenAISearchModel` — no longer needed; any model supports web search via tools\n\n**Removed env vars:**\n- `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` — search model is the same as the main model\n\n### Orchestrator Simplification\n\nThe search sub-agent no longer needs a separate model. In `BuildAgent()`:\n\n```go\n// Before: separate model for search\nsearchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, LLMConfig{\n    Provider: \"openai\", Model: \"gpt-5-search-api\", ...\n})\n\n// After: same model, web search is just a tool\nsearchAgent = llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n    Model: llmModel,  // same model as root/data agent\n    Tools: []tool.Tool{oaimodel.WebSearch{SearchContextSize: \"medium\"}},\n})\n```\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Streaming support (not implemented today, stays that way)\n- Deep research models (o3-deep-research etc.)\n- Gemini adapter changes (unaffected)\n"
}
```

# OpenAI Responses API - Request & Response Format

## Authentication & Headers

Same as Chat Completions:
```
Authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY
Content-Type: application/json
```

Endpoint: `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/responses`

---

## 1. Request Body Structure

### Basic Request

```json
{
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "input": "string or array of input items",
  "tools": [],
  "tool_choice": "auto",
  "instructions": "optional system-level instructions",
  "reasoning": { "effort": "low" | "medium" | "high" },
  "include": ["web_search_call.action.sources"],
  "previous_response_id": "resp_abc123"
}
```

### Input Formats

**Simple string input:**
```json
{
  "input": "What was a positive news story from today?"
}
```

**Structured message array input:**
```json
{
  "input": [
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": "What was a positive news story from today?"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Multi-part content (text + images):**
```json
{
  "input": [
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": [
        {
          "type": "input_text",
          "text": "Describe this image"
        },
        {
          "type": "input_image",
          "image_url": "https://example.com/image.png"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

### Tool Definitions

#### Function Tool
```json
{
  "tools": [
    {
      "type": "function",
      "name": "get_weather",
      "description": "Get the current weather for a location",
      "parameters": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "location": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "City and state, e.g. San Francisco, CA"
          },
          "unit": {
            "type": "string",
            "enum": ["celsius", "fahrenheit"]
          }
        },
        "required": ["location"]
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

#### Web Search Tool (full options)
```json
{
  "tools": [
    {
      "type": "web_search",
      "search_context_size": "low" | "medium" | "high",
      "user_location": {
        "type": "approximate",
        "country": "GB",
        "city": "London",
        "region": "London",
        "timezone": "Europe/London"
      },
      "filters": {
        "allowed_domains": [
          "pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov",
          "clinicaltrials.gov",
          "www.who.int"
        ]
      },
      "external_web_access": true
    }
  ]
}
```

Key notes from the docs:
- `filters` supports up to 100 URLs in the allow-list
- "When formatting URLs, omit the HTTP or HTTPS prefix"
- `external_web_access: false` runs in "offline/cache‑only mode"
- `user_location` uses two-letter ISO country codes and IANA timezones
- "user location is not supported for deep research models"

#### Web Search Preview Tool (legacy)
```json
{
  "tools": [
    {
      "type": "web_search_preview"
    }
  ]
}
```

### Multi-turn with previous_response_id

```json
{
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "input": [
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": "Follow-up question here"
    }
  ],
  "previous_response_id": "resp_abc123def456"
}
```

### Sending Function Call Results Back

When the model returns a function_call output, you send results back as input items:

```json
{
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "input": [
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": "What's the weather in London?"
    },
    {
      "type": "function_call",
      "id": "fc_abc123",
      "call_id": "call_abc123",
      "name": "get_weather",
      "arguments": "{\"location\": \"London, UK\"}"
    },
    {
      "type": "function_call_output",
      "call_id": "call_abc123",
      "output": "{\"temperature\": 15, \"condition\": \"cloudy\"}"
    }
  ],
  "tools": [
    {
      "type": "function",
      "name": "get_weather",
      "description": "Get weather",
      "parameters": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "location": { "type": "string" }
        },
        "required": ["location"]
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

---

## 2. Response Body Structure

### Top-level Response Object

```json
{
  "id": "resp_67c9fa0502748190b7dd390736892e10",
  "object": "response",
  "created_at": 1741214213,
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "status": "completed",
  "output": [],
  "output_text": "The full text output concatenated...",
  "usage": {
    "input_tokens": 150,
    "output_tokens": 320,
    "total_tokens": 470,
    "input_tokens_details": {
      "cached_tokens": 0
    },
    "output_tokens_details": {
      "reasoning_tokens": 0
    }
  },
  "metadata": {},
  "temperature": 1.0,
  "top_p": 1.0,
  "max_output_tokens": null,
  "previous_response_id": null,
  "reasoning": { "effort": "medium" },
  "tools": [],
  "tool_choice": "auto"
}
```

### Output Array Item Types

#### Web Search Call Output Item
```json
{
  "type": "web_search_call",
  "id": "ws_67c9fa0502748190b7dd390736892e100be649c1a5ff9609",
  "status": "completed",
  "action": {
    "type": "search",
    "queries": ["positive news today March 2025"],
    "sources": [
      {
        "url": "https://example.com/article",
        "title": "Article Title",
        "snippet": "Brief excerpt..."
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The `action` field has three types based on the docs:
- `"search"` — "represents a web search" with optional `queries`
- `"open_page"` — "represents a page being opened. Supported in reasoning models"
- `"find_in_page"` — "represents searching within a page. Supported in reasoning models"

Note: `sources` only appear when you pass `"include": ["web_search_call.action.sources"]`.

#### Function Call Output Item
```json
{
  "type": "function_call",
  "id": "fc_67c9fa0502748190b7dd3907",
  "call_id": "call_67c9fa0502748190b7dd3907",
  "name": "get_weather",
  "arguments": "{\"location\": \"London, UK\"}",
  "status": "completed"
}
```

#### Message Output Item (with annotations/citations)
```json
{
  "id": "msg_67c9fa077e288190af08fdffda2e34f20be649c1a5ff9609",
  "type": "message",
  "status": "completed",
  "role": "assistant",
  "content": [
    {
      "type": "output_text",
      "text": "On March 6, 2025, several news...",
      "annotations": [
        {
          "type": "url_citation",
          "start_index": 2606,
          "end_index": 2758,
          "url": "https://example.com/article",
          "title": "Article Title"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

### Complete Response Example (Web Search)

```json
{
  "id": "resp_67c9fa0502748190",
  "object": "response",
  "created_at": 1741214213,
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "status": "completed",
  "output": [
    {
      "type": "web_search_call",
      "id": "ws_67c9fa0502748190b7dd390736892e100be649c1a5ff9609",
      "status": "completed"
    },
    {
      "id": "msg_67c9fa077e288190af08fdffda2e34f20be649c1a5ff9609",
      "type": "message",
      "status": "completed",
      "role": "assistant",
      "content": [
        {
          "type": "output_text",
          "text": "On March 6, 2025, several news...",
          "annotations": [
            {
              "type": "url_citation",
              "start_index": 2606,
              "end_index": 2758,
              "url": "https://...",
              "title": "Title..."
            }
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "output_text": "On March 6, 2025, several news...",
  "usage": {
    "input_tokens": 150,
    "output_tokens": 320,
    "total_tokens": 470
  }
}
```

---

## 3. Key Differences from Chat Completions

| Aspect | Chat Completions | Responses API |
|--------|-----------------|---------------|
| Endpoint | `/v1/chat/completions` | `/v1/responses` |
| Auth | Same `Bearer` token | Same `Bearer` token |
| Input format | `messages` array with `role`/`content` | `input` (string or array) |
| Multi-turn | Manually pass full message history | Use `previous_response_id` |
| Web search | Requires special models (`gpt-4o-search-preview`) with `web_search_options` | Any compatible model + `tools: [{"type": "web_search"}]` |
| Tool results | `tool` role messages | `function_call_output` input items matched by `call_id` |
| Response structure | `choices[0].message.content` | `output` array + `output_text` convenience field |
| Domain filtering | Not available | `filters.allowed_domains` (up to 100) |
| Sources | Not available | Via `include: ["web_search_call.action.sources"]` |
| Live access control | Not available | `external_web_access: true/false` |
```

## Responses API Function Calling: Multi-Turn Format

### 1. Echoing function_call items + adding outputs

Yes, you need **both**. The docs show appending the entire `response.output` (which includes function_call items), then adding output items:

```python
input_list += response.output

for item in response.output:
    if item.type == "function_call":
        input_list.append({
            "type": "function_call_output",
            "call_id": item.call_id,
            "output": json.dumps({"horoscope": horoscope})
        })
```

The pattern is: accumulate a running input list, append all output items from the response, then append your `function_call_output` entries matched by `call_id`.

### 2. `previous_response_id` approach

The page content provided does not mention a `previous_response_id` parameter for the Responses API function calling flow. The documented approach uses a "running input list" that you build up over time.

### 3. Exact format of `function_call_output`

```json
{
    "type": "function_call_output",
    "call_id": "call_12345xyz",
    "output": "{\"temperature\": \"25\", \"unit\": \"C\"}"
}
```

The `output` field is typically a string (JSON-encoded or plain text). For functions returning images/files, you can pass "an array of image or file objects instead of a string."

### 4. Tool definition structure in Responses API

In the Responses API, `description` is at the **top level** alongside `type` and `name` — there is **no** nested `function` key. Compare:

**Responses API format:**
```json
{
    "type": "function",
    "name": "get_horoscope",
    "description": "Get today's horoscope for an astrological sign.",
    "parameters": { ... }
}
```

**Chat Completions API format** (different — has nested `function` key):
```json
{
    "type": "function",
    "function": {
        "name": "get_horoscope",
        "description": "Get today's horoscope...",
        "parameters": { ... },
        "strict": true
    }
}
```

This is a key structural difference between the two APIs.

> AGENT

Now I understand the full picture. ADK passes `req.Contents` (the conversation history) and `req.Config` (system instruction, tools, generation params) to `GenerateContent`. The adapter needs to convert these to the Responses API format.

Key difference: In the Responses API multi-turn flow, function_call items from the model's output must be echoed back as input items before the function_call_output. The ADK already handles this — it sends FunctionCall parts followed by FunctionResponse parts in `req.Contents`. We just need to map them to the Responses API format.

Let me write the implementation plan now.
