It adds a GoogleSearch marker to the ADK\n// request config, which the OpenAI adapter translates into a\n// {\"type\": \"web_search\", ...} tool entry.\ntype WebSearch struct {\n\tSearchContextSize string   // \"low\", \"medium\" (default), \"high\"\n\tAllowedDomains    []string // optional domain filter (up to 100)\n}\n\n// Name implements tool.Tool.\nfunc (WebSearch) Name() string { return \"web_search\" }\n\n// Description implements tool.Tool.\nfunc (WebSearch) Description() string {\n\treturn \"Search the web for additional context about a release.\"\n}\n\n// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.\nfunc (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool { return false }\n\n// ProcessRequest implements toolinternal.RequestProcessor by adding a\n// GoogleSearch marker to the LLM request. The OpenAI model wrapper\n// detects this marker and emits a web_search tool in the Responses API body.\nfunc (ws WebSearch) ProcessRequest(_ tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {\n\tif req == nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"llm request is nil\")\n\t}\n\tif req.Config == nil {\n\t\treq.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}\n\t}\n\treq.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{\n\t\tGoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},\n\t})\n\treturn nil\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests**\n\nRun: `go test -v -run TestWebSearch ./internal/agent/openai/...`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/agent/openai/websearch.go internal/agent/openai/websearch_test.go\ngit commit -m \"feat(agent/openai): add config fields to WebSearch tool\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 2: Rewrite OpenAI adapter request types for Responses API\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`\n\n**Step 1: Replace Chat Completions types with Responses API types**\n\nReplace the type definitions in `openai.go`. Remove `chatRequest`, `chatMessage`, `chatTool`, `chatFunction`, `chatResponse`, `chatChoice`, `chatUsage`, `apiError`, `webSearchOptions`, `toolCall`, `functionCall`.\n\nAdd new types:\n\n```go\n// --- Responses API types ---\n\n// responsesRequest is the body for POST /v1/responses.\ntype responsesRequest struct {\n\tModel        string         `json:\"model\"`\n\tInput        []inputItem    `json:\"input\"`\n\tInstructions string         `json:\"instructions,omitempty\"`\n\tTools        []responseTool `json:\"tools,omitempty\"`\n\tTemperature  *float32       `json:\"temperature,omitempty\"`\n\tTopP         *float32       `json:\"top_p,omitempty\"`\n\tMaxTokens    int32          `json:\"max_output_tokens,omitempty\"`\n\tStore        bool           `json:\"store\"`\n}\n\n// inputItem is a union type for input array entries.\n// Only one of the \"type\" variants is populated per item.\ntype inputItem struct {\n\t// Message input (role + content)\n\tType    string `json:\"type\"`              // \"message\", \"function_call\", \"function_call_output\"\n\tRole    string `json:\"role,omitempty\"`     // \"user\", \"assistant\", \"system\", \"developer\"\n\tContent string `json:\"content,omitempty\"` // for message and function_call_output\n\n\t// function_call echo fields\n\tID        string `json:\"id,omitempty\"`\n\tCallID    string `json:\"call_id,omitempty\"`\n\tName      string `json:\"name,omitempty\"`\n\tArguments string `json:\"arguments,omitempty\"`\n\n\t// function_call_output fields\n\t// CallID and Content are reused\n\tOutput string `json:\"output,omitempty\"` // function result\n}\n\n// responseTool represents a tool in the Responses API tools array.\ntype responseTool struct {\n\tType string `json:\"type\"` // \"function\" or \"web_search\"\n\n\t// Function tool fields (type=function)\n\tName        string `json:\"name,omitempty\"`\n\tDescription string `json:\"description,omitempty\"`\n\tParameters  any    `json:\"parameters,omitempty\"`\n\n\t// Web search fields (type=web_search)\n\tSearchContextSize string         `json:\"search_context_size,omitempty\"`\n\tUserLocation      any            `json:\"user_location,omitempty\"`\n\tFilters           *searchFilters `json:\"filters,omitempty\"`\n}\n\ntype searchFilters struct {\n\tAllowedDomains []string `json:\"allowed_domains,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// responsesResponse is the top-level response from POST /v1/responses.\ntype responsesResponse struct {\n\tID         string       `json:\"id\"`\n\tStatus     string       `json:\"status\"`\n\tOutput     []outputItem `json:\"output\"`\n\tOutputText string       `json:\"output_text\"`\n\tUsage      *respUsage   `json:\"usage,omitempty\"`\n\tError      *apiError    `json:\"error,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// outputItem is a union type for output array entries.\ntype outputItem struct {\n\tType   string `json:\"type\"` // \"message\", \"function_call\", \"web_search_call\"\n\tID     string `json:\"id,omitempty\"`\n\tStatus string `json:\"status,omitempty\"`\n\n\t// function_call fields\n\tCallID    string `json:\"call_id,omitempty\"`\n\tName      string `json:\"name,omitempty\"`\n\tArguments string `json:\"arguments,omitempty\"`\n\n\t// message fields\n\tRole    string          `json:\"role,omitempty\"`\n\tContent []outputContent `json:\"content,omitempty\"`\n}\n\ntype outputContent struct {\n\tType        string       `json:\"type\"` // \"output_text\"\n\tText        string       `json:\"text\"`\n\tAnnotations []annotation `json:\"annotations,omitempty\"`\n}\n\ntype annotation struct {\n\tType       string `json:\"type\"` // \"url_citation\"\n\tURL        string `json:\"url\"`\n\tTitle      string `json:\"title\"`\n\tStartIndex int    `json:\"start_index\"`\n\tEndIndex   int    `json:\"end_index\"`\n}\n\ntype respUsage struct {\n\tInputTokens  int32 `json:\"input_tokens\"`\n\tOutputTokens int32 `json:\"output_tokens\"`\n\tTotalTokens  int32 `json:\"total_tokens\"`\n}\n\ntype apiError struct {\n\tMessage string `json:\"message\"`\n\tType    string `json:\"type\"`\n\tCode    string `json:\"code\"`\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `go build ./internal/agent/openai/...`\nExpected: compilation errors because `doRequest`, This is expected — we fix them in Task 3.\n\n---\n\n### Task 3: Rewrite conversion functions for Responses API\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`\n\n**Step 1: Write tests for \"high\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected search_context_size 'high', got %q\", searchTool.SearchContextSize)\n\t}\n\tif searchTool.Filters == nil || len(searchTool.Filters.AllowedDomains) != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected 1 allowed domain\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestConvertResponseOutput_TextAndFunctionCalls(t *testing.T) {\n\tresp := &responsesResponse{\n\t\tOutput: yet.\n\n**Step 3: Implement conversion functions**\n\nReplace `convertContents`, `convertTools`, `convertResponse`, `hasWebSearch`, `mergeAssistantToolCalls` with:\n\n```go\n// convertInputs transforms ADK genai.Content messages into Responses API input items.\n// Returns the input items and extracted system instructions.\nfunc convertInputs(req *model.LLMRequest) ([]inputItem, string) {\n\tvar items []inputItem\n\tvar instructions string\n\n\t// Extract system instruction separately (Responses API uses \"instructions\" field).\n\tif req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil {\n\t\tvar text string\n\t\tfor _, p := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {\n\t\t\tif p.Text != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\ttext += p.Text\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tinstructions = text\n\t}\n\n\t// Conversation contents.\n\tfor _, c := range req.Contents {\n\t\trole := mapRole(c.Role)\n\n\t\tfor _, p := range c.Parts {\n\t\t\tswitch {\n\t\t\tcase p.FunctionCall != nil:\n\t\t\t\targsJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionCall.Args)\n\t\t\t\titems = append(items, inputItem{\n\t\t\t\t\tType:      \"function_call\",\n\t\t\t\t\tCallID:    p.FunctionCall.ID,\n\t\t\t\t\tName:      p.FunctionCall.Name,\n\t\t\t\t\tArguments: string(argsJSON),\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tcase p.FunctionResponse != nil:\n\t\t\t\trespJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionResponse.Response)\n\t\t\t\titems = append(items, inputItem{\n\t\t\t\t\tType:   \"function_call_output\",\n\t\t\t\t\tCallID: p.FunctionResponse.ID,\n\t\t\t\t\tOutput: string(respJSON),\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tcase p.Text != \"\":\n\t\t\t\titems = append(items, inputItem{\n\t\t\t\t\tType:    \"message\",\n\t\t\t\t\tRole:    role,\n\t\t\t\t\tContent: p.Text,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn items, instructions\n}\n\n// webSearchConfig extracts web search parameters from the WebSearch tool\n// registered on the model. Returns nil if no WebSearch is configured.\nfunc (m *openaiModel) webSearchConfig() *WebSearch {\n\treturn m.wsConfig\n}\n\n// convertResponseTools transforms ADK genai.Tool definitions into Responses API tools.\n// If wsConfig is non-nil and a GoogleSearch marker is present, it emits a web_search\n// tool with the configured parameters.\nfunc convertResponseTools(req *model.LLMRequest, wsConfig *WebSearch) []responseTool {\n\tif req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tvar tools []responseTool\n\thasSearch := false\n\n\tfor _, t := range req.Config.Tools {\n\t\tif t.GoogleSearch != nil {\n\t\t\thasSearch = true\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {\n\t\t\trt := responseTool{\n\t\t\t\tType:        \"function\",\n\t\t\t\tName:        fd.Name,\n\t\t\t\tDescription: fd.Description,\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif fd.Parameters != nil {\n\t\t\t\trt.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ttools = append(tools, rt)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif hasSearch {\n\t\twsTool := responseTool{Type: \"web_search\"}\n\t\tif wsConfig != nil {\n\t\t\tif wsConfig.SearchContextSize != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\twsTool.SearchContextSize = wsConfig.SearchContextSize\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif len(wsConfig.AllowedDomains) > 0 {\n\t\t\t\twsTool.Filters = &searchFilters{\n\t\t\t\t\tAllowedDomains: wsConfig.AllowedDomains,\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\ttools = append(tools, wsTool)\n\t}\n\n\treturn tools\n}\n\n// convertResponseOutput transforms a Responses API response into an ADK LLMResponse.\nfunc convertResponseOutput(resp *responsesResponse) *model.LLMResponse {\n\tllmResp := &model.LLMResponse{\n\t\tTurnComplete: true,\n\t}\n\n\tcontent := &genai.Content{Role: \"model\"}\n\n\tfor _, item := range resp.Output {\n\t\tswitch item.Type {\n\t\tcase \"function_call\":\n\t\t\tvar args map[string]any\n\t\t\tif item.Arguments != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\t_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(item.Arguments), &args)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontent.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{\n\t\t\t\tFunctionCall: &genai.FunctionCall{\n\t\t\t\t\tID:   item.CallID,\n\t\t\t\t\tName: item.Name,\n\t\t\t\t\tArgs: args,\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\tllmResp.FinishReason = genai.FinishReasonStop\n\n\t\tcase \"message\":\n\t\t\tfor _, c := range item.Content {\n\t\t\t\tif c.Text != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\t\tcontent.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: c.Text})\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tllmResp.FinishReason = genai.FinishReasonStop\n\n\t\tcase \"web_search_call\":\n\t\t\t// Informational; no ADK equivalent. Skip.\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif len(content.Parts) == 0 {\n\t\tcontent.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: \"\"})\n\t}\n\n\tllmResp.Content = content\n\n\tif resp.Usage != nil {\n\t\tllmResp.UsageMetadata = &genai.GenerateContentResponseUsageMetadata{\n\t\t\tPromptTokenCount:     resp.Usage.InputTokens,\n\t\t\tCandidatesTokenCount: resp.Usage.OutputTokens,\n\t\t\tTotalTokenCount:      resp.Usage.TotalTokens,\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn llmResp\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests**\n\nRun: `go test -v -run \"TestConvertInputs|TestConvertResponseTools|TestConvertResponseOutput|TestSchemaToMap\" ./internal/agent/openai/...`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add model struct for Responses API\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`\n\n**Step 1: Update openaiModel struct and NewModel to accept WebSearch config**\n\n```go\ntype openaiModel struct {\n\tname     string\n\tapiKey   string\n\tbaseURL  string\n\tclient   *http.Client\n\twsConfig *WebSearch // optional web search config\n}\n\n// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.\n// wsConfig is optional; pass nil to disable web search.\nfunc NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config, wsConfig *WebSearch) (model.LLM, error) {\n\tif cfg.APIKey == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: API key is required\")\n\t}\n\tbaseURL := cfg.BaseURL\n\tif baseURL == \"\" {\n\t\tbaseURL = defaultBaseURL\n\t}\n\treturn &openaiModel{\n\t\tname:     modelName,\n\t\tapiKey:   cfg.APIKey,\n\t\tbaseURL:  baseURL,\n\t\tclient:   &http.Client{},\n\t\twsConfig: wsConfig,\n\t}, nil\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Rewrite doRequest for Responses API**\n\n```go\nfunc (m *openaiModel) doRequest(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {\n\titems, instructions := convertInputs(req)\n\ttools := convertResponseTools(req, m.wsConfig)\n\n\tapiReq := responsesRequest{\n\t\tModel:        m.name,\n\t\tInput:        items,\n\t\tInstructions: instructions,\n\t\tTools:        tools,\n\t\tStore:        false,\n\t}\n\n\tif req.Config != nil {\n\t\tapiReq.Temperature = req.Config.Temperature\n\t\tapiReq.TopP = req.Config.TopP\n\t\tif req.Config.MaxOutputTokens > 0 {\n\t\t\tapiReq.MaxTokens = req.Config.MaxOutputTokens\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tbody, err := json.Marshal(apiReq)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: marshal request: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\thttpReq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, m.baseURL+\"/responses\", bytes.NewReader(body))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: create http request: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\thttpReq.Header.Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\thttpReq.Header.Set(\"Authorization\", \"Bearer \"+m.apiKey)\n\n\thttpResp, err := m.client.Do(httpReq)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: http request: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer httpResp.Body.Close()\n\n\trespBody, err := io.ReadAll(httpResp.Body)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: read response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif httpResp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: API returned status %d: %s\", httpResp.StatusCode, string(respBody))\n\t}\n\n\tvar apiResp responsesResponse\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(respBody, &apiResp); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: unmarshal response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif apiResp.Error != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"openai: API error: %s (type=%s, code=%s)\",\n\t\t\tapiResp.Error.Message, apiResp.Error.Type, apiResp.Error.Code)\n\t}\n\n\treturn convertResponseOutput(&apiResp), nil\n}\n```\n\n**Step 3: Remove old dead code**\n\nDelete these functions that are no longer used:\n- `convertContents`\n- Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.\",\n\t\tModel:       llmModel,\n\t\tTools:       functionTools,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create data sub-agent: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Search sub-agent: provider-specific web search.\n\tvar searchTool tool.Tool\n\tvar searchModel model.LLM\n\tswitch llmConfig.Provider {\n\tcase \"openai\":\n\t\twsConfig := &oaimodel.WebSearch{SearchContextSize: \"medium\"}\n\t\tsearchTool = *wsConfig\n\t\t// Same model, web search is a native tool in the Responses API.\n\t\tsearchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig, wsConfig)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create OpenAI search model: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\tdefault: // gemini\n\t\tsearchTool = geminitool.GoogleSearch{}\n\t\tsearchModel = llmModel\n\t}\n\n\tsearchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n\t\tName:        \"search_agent\",\n\t\tDescription: \"Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.\",\n\t\tModel:       searchModel,\n\t\tTools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create search sub-agent: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.\n\treturn llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n\t\tName:        \"release_analyst\",\n\t\tDescription: \"Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.\",\n\t\tModel:       llmModel,\n\t\tInstruction: instruction,\n\t\tTools: []tool.Tool{\n\t\t\tagenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),\n\t\t\tagenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),\n\t\t},\n\t})\n}\n```\n\nRemove the `geminitool` import if no longer needed after this change.      1→# OpenAI Responses API Migration — Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Migrate the OpenAI adapter from Chat Completions API to Responses API for native web search tool support with domain filtering and search context control.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Replace `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `POST /v1/responses` in the OpenAI adapter. The Responses API uses a different wire format (`input` items instead of `messages`, flat tool definitions instead of nested `function` wrappers, `function_call`/`function_call_output` input items instead of `tool` role messages). The WebSearch tool gains config fields (SearchContextSize, AllowedDomains). The search sub-agent no longer needs a separate model.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** Go, `net/http`, Google ADK-Go v0.5.0 (`model.LLM` interface)
    10→
    11→**Design doc:** `docs/plans/2026-03-04-openai-responses-api-design.md`
    12→

    14→

    83→**Step 3: Update WebSearch struct with config fields**
    84→
    85→In `websearch.go`, add the configurable fields:
    86→
    87→```go
    88→package openai
    89→
    90→import (
    91→	"fmt"
    92→
    93→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    94→
    95→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    96→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    97→)
    98→
    99→// WebSearch is a tool that enables OpenAI's built-in web search via the
   100→// Responses API tools array. It adds a GoogleSearch marker to the ADK
   101→// request config, which the OpenAI adapter translates into a
   102→// {"type": "web_search", ...} tool entry.
   103→type WebSearch struct {
   104→	SearchContextSize string   // "low", "medium" (default), "high"
   105→	AllowedDomains    []string // optional domain filter (up to 100)
   106→}
   107→
   108→// Name implements tool.Tool.
   109→func (WebSearch) Name() string { return "web_search" }
   110→
   111→// Description implements tool.Tool.
   112→func (WebSearch) Description() string {
   113→	return "Search the web for additional context about a release."
   114→}
   115→
   116→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
   117→func (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool { return false }
   118→
   119→// ProcessRequest implements toolinternal.RequestProcessor by adding a
   120→// GoogleSearch marker to the LLM request. The OpenAI model wrapper
   121→// detects this marker and emits a web_search tool in the Responses API body.
   122→func (ws WebSearch) ProcessRequest(_ tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   123→	if req == nil {
   124→		return fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")
   125→	}
   126→	if req.Config == nil {
   127→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
   128→	}
   129→	req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
   130→		GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},
   131→	})
   132→	return nil
   133→}
   134→```

   150→### Task 2: Rewrite OpenAI adapter request types for Responses API
   151→
   152→**Files:**
   153→- Modify: `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`
   154→
   155→**Step 1: Replace Chat Completions types with Responses API types**
   156→
   157→Replace the type definitions in `openai.go`. Remove `chatRequest`, `chatMessage`, `chatTool`, `chatFunction`, `chatResponse`, `chatChoice`, `chatUsage`, `apiError`, `webSearchOptions`, `toolCall`, `functionCall`.
   158→
   159→Add new types:
   160→
   161→```go
   162→// --- Responses API types ---
   163→
   164→// responsesRequest is the body for POST /v1/responses.
   165→type responsesRequest struct {
   166→	Model        string         `json:"model"`
   167→	Input        []inputItem    `json:"input"`
   168→	Instructions string         `json:"instructions,omitempty"`
   169→	Tools        []responseTool `json:"tools,omitempty"`
   170→	Temperature  *float32       `json:"temperature,omitempty"`
   171→	TopP         *float32       `json:"top_p,omitempty"`
   172→	MaxTokens    int32          `json:"max_output_tokens,omitempty"`
   173→	Store        bool           `json:"store"`
   174→}
   175→
   176→// inputItem is a union type for input array entries.
   177→// Only one of the "type" variants is populated per item.
   178→type inputItem struct {
   179→	// Message input (role + content)
   180→	Type    string `json:"type"`              // "message", "function_call", "function_call_output"
   181→	Role    string `json:"role,omitempty"`     // "user", "assistant", "system", "developer"
   182→	Content string `json:"content,omitempty"` // for message and function_call_output
   183→
   184→	// function_call echo fields
   185→	ID        string `json:"id,omitempty"`
   186→	CallID    string `json:"call_id,omitempty"`
   187→	Name      string `json:"name,omitempty"`
   188→	Arguments string `json:"arguments,omitempty"`
   189→
   190→	// function_call_output fields
   191→	// CallID and Content are reused
   192→	Output string `json:"output,omitempty"` // function result
   193→}
   194→
   195→// responseTool represents a tool in the Responses API tools array.
   196→type responseTool struct {
   197→	Type string `json:"type"` // "function" or "web_search"
   198→
   199→	// Function tool fields (type=function)
   200→	Name        string `json:"name,omitempty"`
   201→	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
   202→	Parameters  any    `json:"parameters,omitempty"`
   203→
   204→	// Web search fields (type=web_search)
   205→	SearchContextSize string         `json:"search_context_size,omitempty"`
   206→	UserLocation      any            `json:"user_location,omitempty"`
   207→	Filters           *searchFilters `json:"filters,omitempty"`
   208→}
   209→
   210→type searchFilters struct {
   211→	AllowedDomains []string `json:"allowed_domains,omitempty"`
   212→}
   213→
   214→// responsesResponse is the top-level response from POST /v1/responses.
   215→type responsesResponse struct {
   216→	ID         string       `json:"id"`
   217→	Status     string       `json:"status"`
   218→	Output     []outputItem `json:"output"`
   219→	OutputText string       `json:"output_text"`
   220→	Usage      *respUsage   `json:"usage,omitempty"`
   221→	Error      *apiError    `json:"error,omitempty"`
   222→}
   223→
   224→// outputItem is a union type for output array entries.
   225→type outputItem struct {
   226→	Type   string `json:"type"` // "message", "function_call", "web_search_call"
   227→	ID     string `json:"id,omitempty"`
   228→	Status string `json:"status,omitempty"`
   229→
   230→	// function_call fields
   231→	CallID    string `json:"call_id,omitempty"`
   232→	Name      string `json:"name,omitempty"`
   233→	Arguments string `json:"arguments,omitempty"`
   234→
   235→	// message fields
   236→	Role    string          `json:"role,omitempty"`
   237→	Content []outputContent `json:"content,omitempty"`
   238→}
   239→
   240→type outputContent struct {
   241→	Type        string       `json:"type"` // "output_text"
   242→	Text        string       `json:"text"`
   243→	Annotations []annotation `json:"annotations,omitempty"`
   244→}
   245→
   246→type annotation struct {
   247→	Type       string `json:"type"` // "url_citation"
   248→	URL        string `json:"url"`
   249→	Title      string `json:"title"`
   250→	StartIndex int    `json:"start_index"`
   251→	EndIndex   int    `json:"end_index"`
   252→}
   253→
   254→type respUsage struct {
   255→	InputTokens  int32 `json:"input_tokens"`
   256→	OutputTokens int32 `json:"output_tokens"`
   257→	TotalTokens  int32 `json:"total_tokens"`
   258→}
   259→
   260→type apiError struct {
   261→	Message string `json:"message"`
   262→	Type    string `json:"type"`
   263→	Code    string `json:"code"`
   264→}
   265→```
   266→

   447→**Step 3: Implement conversion functions**
   448→
   449→Replace `convertContents`, `convertTools`, `convertResponse`, `hasWebSearch`, `mergeAssistantToolCalls` with:
   450→
   451→```go
   452→// convertInputs transforms ADK genai.Content messages into Responses API input items.
   453→// Returns the input items and extracted system instructions.
   454→func convertInputs(req *model.LLMRequest) ([]inputItem, string) {
   455→	var items []inputItem
   456→	var instructions string
   457→
   458→	// Extract system instruction separately (Responses API uses "instructions" field).
   459→	if req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil {
   460→		var text string
   461→		for _, p := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {
   462→			if p.Text != "" {
   463→				text += p.Text
   464→			}
   465→		}
   466→		instructions = text
   467→	}
   468→
   469→	// Conversation contents.
   470→	for _, c := range req.Contents {
   471→		role := mapRole(c.Role)
   472→
   473→		for _, p := range c.Parts {
   474→			switch {
   475→			case p.FunctionCall != nil:
   476→				argsJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionCall.Args)
   477→				items = append(items, inputItem{
   478→					Type:      "function_call",
   479→					CallID:    p.FunctionCall.ID,
   480→					Name:      p.FunctionCall.Name,
   481→					Arguments: string(argsJSON),
   482→				})
   483→			case p.FunctionResponse != nil:
   484→				respJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionResponse.Response)
   485→				items = append(items, inputItem{
   486→					Type:   "function_call_output",
   487→					CallID: p.FunctionResponse.ID,
   488→					Output: string(respJSON),
   489→				})
   490→			case p.Text != "":
   491→				items = append(items, inputItem{
   492→					Type:    "message",
   493→					Role:    role,
   494→					Content: p.Text,
   495→				})
   496→			}
   497→		}
   498→	}
   499→
   500→	return items, instructions
   501→}
   502→
   503→// webSearchConfig extracts web search parameters from the WebSearch tool
   504→// registered on the model. Returns nil if no WebSearch is configured.
   505→func (m *openaiModel) webSearchConfig() *WebSearch {
   506→	return m.wsConfig
   507→}
   508→
   509→// convertResponseTools transforms ADK genai.Tool definitions into Responses API tools.
   510→// If wsConfig is non-nil and a GoogleSearch marker is present, it emits a web_search
   511→// tool with the configured parameters.
   512→func convertResponseTools(req *model.LLMRequest, wsConfig *WebSearch) []responseTool {
   513→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   514→		return nil
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	var tools []responseTool
   518→	hasSearch := false
   519→
   520→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   521→		if t.GoogleSearch != nil {
   522→			hasSearch = true
   523→			continue
   524→		}
   525→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   526→			rt := responseTool{
   527→				Type:        "function",
   528→				Name:        fd.Name,
   529→				Description: fd.Description,
   530→			}
   531→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   532→				rt.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   533→			}
   534→			tools = append(tools, rt)
   535→		}
   536→	}
   537→
   538→	if hasSearch {
   539→		wsTool := responseTool{Type: "web_search"}
   540→		if wsConfig != nil {
   541→			if wsConfig.SearchContextSize != "" {
   542→				wsTool.SearchContextSize = wsConfig.SearchContextSize
   543→			}
   544→			if len(wsConfig.AllowedDomains) > 0 {
   545→				wsTool.Filters = &searchFilters{
   546→					AllowedDomains: wsConfig.AllowedDomains,
   547→				}
   548→			}
   549→		}
   550→		tools = append(tools, wsTool)
   551→	}
   552→
   553→	return tools
   554→}
   555→
   556→// convertResponseOutput transforms a Responses API response into an ADK LLMResponse.
   557→func convertResponseOutput(resp *responsesResponse) *model.LLMResponse {
   558→	llmResp := &model.LLMResponse{
   559→		TurnComplete: true,
   560→	}
   561→
   562→	content := &genai.Content{Role: "model"}
   563→
   564→	for _, item := range resp.Output {
   565→		switch item.Type {
   566→		case "function_call":
   567→			var args map[string]any
   568→			if item.Arguments != "" {
   569→				_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(item.Arguments), &args)
   570→			}
   571→			content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{
   572→				FunctionCall: &genai.FunctionCall{
   573→					ID:   item.CallID,
   574→					Name: item.Name,
   575→					Args: args,
   576→				},
   577→			})
   578→			llmResp.FinishReason = genai.FinishReasonStop
   579→
   580→		case "message":
   581→			for _, c := range item.Content {
   582→				if c.Text != "" {
   583→					content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: c.Text})
   584→				}
   585→			}
   586→			llmResp.FinishReason = genai.FinishReasonStop
   587→
   588→		case "web_search_call":
   589→			// Informational; no ADK equivalent. Skip.
   590→		}
   591→	}
   592→
   593→	if len(content.Parts) == 0 {
   594→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: ""})
   595→	}
   596→
   597→	llmResp.Content = content
   598→
   599→	if resp.Usage != nil {
   600→		llmResp.UsageMetadata = &genai.GenerateContentResponseUsageMetadata{
   601→			PromptTokenCount:     resp.Usage.InputTokens,
   602→			CandidatesTokenCount: resp.Usage.OutputTokens,
   603→			TotalTokenCount:      resp.Usage.TotalTokens,
   604→		}
   605→	}
   606→
   607→	return llmResp
   608→}
   609→```

   625→### Task 4: Rewrite doRequest and model struct for Responses API
   626→
   627→**Files:**
   628→- Modify: `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`
   629→
   630→**Step 1: Update openaiModel struct and NewModel to accept WebSearch config**
   631→
   632→```go
   633→type openaiModel struct {
   634→	name     string
   635→	apiKey   string
   636→	baseURL  string
   637→	client   *http.Client
   638→	wsConfig *WebSearch // optional web search config
   639→}
   640→
   641→// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.
   642→// wsConfig is optional; pass nil to disable web search.
   643→func NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config, wsConfig *WebSearch) (model.LLM, error) {
   644→	if cfg.APIKey == "" {
   645→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API key is required")
   646→	}
   647→	baseURL := cfg.BaseURL
   648→	if baseURL == "" {
   649→		baseURL = defaultBaseURL
   650→	}
   651→	return &openaiModel{
   652→		name:     modelName,
   653→		apiKey:   cfg.APIKey,
   654→		baseURL:  baseURL,
   655→		client:   &http.Client{},
   656→		wsConfig: wsConfig,
   657→	}, nil
   658→}
   659→```
   660→
   661→**Step 2: Rewrite doRequest for Responses API**
   662→
   663→```go
   664→func (m *openaiModel) doRequest(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   665→	items, instructions := convertInputs(req)
   666→	tools := convertResponseTools(req, m.wsConfig)
   667→
   668→	apiReq := responsesRequest{
   669→		Model:        m.name,
   670→		Input:        items,
   671→		Instructions: instructions,
   672→		Tools:        tools,
   673→		Store:        false,
   674→	}
   675→
   676→	if req.Config != nil {
   677→		apiReq.Temperature = req.Config.Temperature
   678→		apiReq.TopP = req.Config.TopP
   679→		if req.Config.MaxOutputTokens > 0 {
   680→			apiReq.MaxTokens = req.Config.MaxOutputTokens
   681→		}
   682→	}
   683→
   684→	body, err := json.Marshal(apiReq)
   685→	if err != nil {
   686→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: marshal request: %w", err)
   687→	}
   688→
   689→	httpReq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, m.baseURL+"/responses", bytes.NewReader(body))
   690→	if err != nil {
   691→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: create http request: %w", err)
   692→	}
   693→	httpReq.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   694→	httpReq.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+m.apiKey)
   695→
   696→	httpResp, err := m.client.Do(httpReq)
   697→	if err != nil {
   698→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: http request: %w", err)
   699→	}
   700→	defer httpResp.Body.Close()
   701→
   702→	respBody, err := io.ReadAll(httpResp.Body)
   703→	if err != nil {
   704→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: read response: %w", err)
   705→	}
   706→
   707→	if httpResp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
   708→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API returned status %d: %s", httpResp.StatusCode, string(respBody))
   709→	}
   710→
   711→	var apiResp responsesResponse
   712→	if err := json.Unmarshal(respBody, &apiResp); err != nil {
   713→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: unmarshal response: %w", err)
   714→	}
   715→
   716→	if apiResp.Error != nil {
   717→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API error: %s (type=%s, code=%s)",
   718→			apiResp.Error.Message, apiResp.Error.Type, apiResp.Error.Code)
   719→	}
   720→
   721→	return convertResponseOutput(&apiResp), nil
   722→}
   723→```

items[2].Type != \"function_call_output\" {\n        t.Errorf(\"item 2: expected function_call_output, got %s\", items[2].Type)\n    }\n}\n\nfunc TestConvertResponseTools_WebSearch(t *testing.T) {\n    ws := &WebSearch{SearchContextSize: \"high\", AllowedDomains: []string{\"github.com\"}}\n    req := &model.LLMRequest{\n        Config: &genai.GenerateContentConfig{\n            Tools: []*genai.Tool{\n                {\n                    FunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{\n                        {Name: \"get_releases\", Description: \"Fetch releases\"},\n convertResponseTools(req, ws)\n\n    if len(tools) != 2 {\n        t.Fatalf(\"expected 2 tools, got %d\", len(tools))\n    }\n\n    var funcTool, searchTool *responseTool\n    for i := range tools {\n nil || funcTool.Name != \"get_releases\" {\n        t.Errorf(\"missing or wrong function tool\")\n    }\n    if searchTool == nil {\n        t.Fatal(\"no web_search tool found\")\n    }\n    if searchTool.SearchContextSize != \"high\" {\n        t.Errorf(\"expected search_context_size 'high', got %q\", searchTool.SearchContextSize)\n    }\n    if searchTool.Filters == nil || The adapter implements `model.LLM` interface which has `GenerateContent(ctx, req, streaming) iter.Seq2[*model.LLMResponse, error]`. The adapter is called by ADK's runner multiple times during an agent run — first for the initial prompt, then for tool call results, etc.\n\nThe key insight: ADK sends the full conversation history in `req.Contents` each time. For the Responses API, we need to convert:\n- `model` role FunctionCall parts → `{type: \"function_call\"}` input items (echo)\n- `model` role FunctionResponse parts → `{type: \"function_call_output\"}` input items\n- text parts → `{type: \"message\"}` input items\n- System instruction → separate `instructions` field\n\nNote: `NewModel` gains a 4th parameter `wsConfig *WebSearch` — this will temporarily break callers in `model.go` but that gets fixed in the next task. ## Changes Made

### Task 1: WebSearch config fields

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go`**
- Added `SearchContextSize string` and `AllowedDomains []string` fields to the `WebSearch` struct
- Updated the doc comment to reference "Responses API" instead of "web_search_options"

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch_test.go`**
- Renamed `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest` to `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest_SetsMarker`
- Test now constructs `WebSearch{SearchContextSize: "high"}` and verifies the field persists alongside the GoogleSearch marker

### Tasks 2-3: Replace types and conversion functions

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`**

Removed old Chat Completions types:
- `webSearchOptions`, `chatRequest`, `chatMessage`, `chatTool`, `chatFunction`, `chatResponse`, `chatChoice`, `chatUsage`, `toolCall`, `functionCall`

Added new Responses API types:
- `responsesRequest`, `inputItem`, `responseTool`, `searchFilters`, `responsesResponse`, `outputItem`, `outputContent`, `annotation`, `respUsage`, `apiError`

Removed old conversion functions:
- `convertContents`, `convertTools`, `convertResponse`, `hasWebSearch`, `mergeAssistantToolCalls`

Added new conversion functions:
- `convertInputs(req) ([]inputItem, string)` -- converts ADK Contents to input items; extracts system instruction as separate `instructions` string
- `convertResponseTools(req, wsConfig) []responseTool` -- converts ADK tools to flat Responses API format; maps GoogleSearch marker to `{type: "web_search"}` with config from wsConfig
- `convertResponseOutput(resp) *model.LLMResponse` -- converts Responses API output items to ADK LLMResponse; handles `function_call`, `message`, and skips `web_search_call` items

Kept unchanged: `schemaToMap`, `mapRole`, `mapFinishReason`

### Task 4: Update model struct and doRequest

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`**
- Added `wsConfig *WebSearch` field to `openaiModel` struct
- Updated `NewModel` signature to `NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config, wsConfig *WebSearch) (model.LLM, error)`
- Rewrote `doRequest` to build `responsesRequest`, POST to `/responses` endpoint, parse `responsesResponse`, and convert via `convertResponseOutput`

### Tests

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go`**

Removed:
- `TestConvertTools_SkipsGoogleSearchMarker`
- `TestHasWebSearch`

Added:
- `TestConvertInputs_SystemInstruction` -- verifies system instruction extraction
- `TestConvertInputs_FunctionCallAndResponse` -- verifies function_call and function_call_output input item conversion
- `TestConvertResponseTools_WebSearch` -- verifies function tools and web_search tool with config
- `TestConvertResponseOutput_TextAndFunctionCalls` -- verifies output conversion of function calls and text messages

Kept: `TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType`

## Tasks 5-6 (plus Task 7 cmd cleanup) -- Completed

### Files Changed

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go`**
- Removed the `OpenAISearchModel` field from `LLMConfig` struct.
- Updated `NewLLMModel` signature to accept a third parameter `wsConfig *oaimodel.WebSearch`.
- The `wsConfig` is forwarded to `oaimodel.NewModel` as its 4th argument, matching the signature established in the previous tasks.
- Added doc comment explaining the optional `wsConfig` parameter.

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`**
- Updated the main model creation call at line 87 to pass `nil` as the third argument: `NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig, nil)`.
- Simplified the OpenAI search sub-agent block (lines 112-123):
  - Creates a `wsConfig := &oaimodel.WebSearch{SearchContextSize: "medium"}`.
  - Uses `*wsConfig` (dereferenced) as the `searchTool` (satisfies `tool.Tool` interface).
  - Creates the search model via `NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig, wsConfig)` -- same model name, just with web search enabled.
  - No longer references `OpenAISearchModel` or creates a separate `LLMConfig` with a different model name.
- The Gemini default case remains unchanged (`geminitool.GoogleSearch{}`).

**`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go`** and **`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go`**
- Removed the `OpenAISearchModel: envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api")` line from the `LLMConfig` literal in both cmd entrypoints. This was necessary because the field was removed from the struct; without this fix the build would fail.

### Verification

---

## Code Review: Tasks 5, 6, and 7

### What Was Done Well

The implementation is clean and well-organized. The `NewLLMModel` signature change is properly documented with a clear comment. The `BuildAgent` logic for creating `wsConfig` and passing it through is idiomatic Go. The `LLMConfig` struct is now leaner without the extraneous `OpenAISearchModel` field.

---

### Check 1: `OpenAISearchModel` completely removed from all source files

**PASS** -- The `LLMConfig` struct in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go:15-25` has no `OpenAISearchModel` field. The `LLMConfig` initialization in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go:71-77` and `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go:67-73` do not reference it.

The only remaining references are in planning/design documents under `docs/plans/`, which is expected -- those are historical records.

---

### Check 2: `NewLLMModel` has correct signature with `wsConfig` param

**PASS** -- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go:30`:

```go
func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig, wsConfig *oaimodel.WebSearch) (model.LLM, error) {
```

The `wsConfig` parameter is correctly typed as `*oaimodel.WebSearch` and is passed through to `oaimodel.NewModel` as the 4th argument at line 44:

```go
return oaimodel.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, oaimodel.Config{
    APIKey:  cfg.OpenAIAPIKey,
    BaseURL: cfg.OpenAIBaseURL,
}, wsConfig)
```

The underlying `oaimodel.NewModel` in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go:36` confirms it accepts `wsConfig *WebSearch` as the 4th parameter.

---

### Check 3: `BuildAgent` passes `nil` for main model, creates `wsConfig` for search

**PASS** -- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go:87`:

```go
llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig, nil)
```

Main model created with `nil` -- correct.

Lines 112-119 handle the OpenAI search path:

```go
case "openai":
    wsConfig := &oaimodel.WebSearch{SearchContextSize: "medium"}
    searchTool = *wsConfig
    searchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig, wsConfig)
```

This correctly creates `wsConfig`, uses `*wsConfig` (value copy) as the `searchTool`, and passes the pointer to `NewLLMModel` for the search model. The Gemini default at lines 120-122 correctly uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` and reuses `llmModel`.

---

### Check 4: No other functions in orchestrator.go were modified

**PASS** -- The functions `NewOrchestrator` (line 189), `RunAgent` (line 215), `executeAgent` (line 258), `checkAllSourcesReady` (line 150), `sendProjectNotifications` (line 434), `parseReport` (line 495), and `defaultSenders` (line 422) are all unchanged from their expected behavior. Only `BuildAgent` was modified, which is exactly what the spec called for.

---

### Check 5: Cmd files no longer reference `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL`

**PASS** -- Neither `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go` nor `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go` contain any reference to `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` or `OpenAISearchModel`.

---

### Issue Found

**Important (should fix):** The `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` environment variable is still documented in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/README.md` at line 61:

```
| `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` | `gpt-5-search-api` | Search-capable model for OpenAI web search sub-agent |
```

This is stale documentation. The env var no longer exists in any Go source code -- reading it will have no effect. This will confuse operators who set it expecting it to do something.

**Recommendation:** Remove line 61 from `README.md` to keep documentation in sync with the code.

---

### Summary

| Check | Result |
| ----- | ------ |
| 1. `OpenAISearchModel` removed from source | PASS |
| 2. `NewLLMModel` signature correct | PASS |
| 3. `BuildAgent` logic correct | PASS |
| 4. No unintended orchestrator changes | PASS |
| 5. Cmd files clean | PASS |
| 6. README.md documentation sync | **FAIL** -- stale env var reference remains |

All spec requirements for tasks 5, 6, and 7 are correctly implemented in the Go source files. The one remaining issue is the stale `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` reference in `README.md` line 61, which should be removed.

All tests pass (exit 0), vet clean (exit 0). The OpenAI Responses API migration is fully implemented. Here's a summary of what was done:

## Summary

**Migrated** the OpenAI adapter from Chat Completions API (`/v1/chat/completions`) to the Responses API (`/v1/responses`).

### Key commits:
- `ac97603` — Core adapter rewrite: new types (`responsesRequest`, `inputItem`, `responseTool`, etc.), new conversion functions (`convertInputs`, `convertResponseTools`, `convertResponseOutput`), `doRequest` now POSTs to `/responses`
- `f539e72` — Cleanup: removed dead `mapFinishReason`, fixed doc comment
- `17acf32` — Removed `OpenAISearchModel` from `LLMConfig`, simplified `BuildAgent` to use same model for search
- `b53b05f` — Removed stale `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` from README
- `dea3701` — Marked design doc as implemented

### What changed:
| Before | After |
|--------|-------|
| Chat Completions API (`/v1/chat/completions`) | Responses API (`/v1/responses`) |
| Separate search model (`gpt-5-search-api`) | Same model with `web_search` tool |
| `web_search_options: {}` (no control) | `{type: "web_search", search_context_size, filters}` |
| `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` env var | Removed — not needed |
| `WebSearch{}` (no config) | `WebSearch{SearchContextSize, AllowedDomains}` |

> AGENT

Found it. Line 75: `agentpkg.BuildAgent(ctx, store, project, llmConfig, "")` — the version is hardcoded as empty string `""`. Then in `BuildAgent`, `strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)` replaces all `{{VERSION}}` placeholders with empty string, producing instructions like "analyzing version  of a software project" and "Focus ONLY on version ." — which confuses the LLM.

This isn't a regression from our changes — this was pre-existing. But let me check if there's a version flag that should exist.

> TOOL

> AGENT

There's no `--version` flag on the agent CLI. The dev CLI passes `""` as the version to `BuildAgent`, which causes the `{{VERSION}}` placeholders to become empty strings in the agent instruction. The LLM then sees "analyzing version  of a software project" and gets confused.

The fix is straightforward — add a `--version` flag to the agent CLI.

> TOOL

  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "\tprojectID := flag.String(\"project-id\", \"\", \"Project ID to scope the agent to (required)\")\n\tversion := flag.String(\"version\", \"\", \"Target version to analyze (e.g. v1.10.15)\")\n\tflag.Parse()"
}

> AGENT

The instruction is version-centric — every sentence references `{{VERSION}}`. When version is empty, it becomes nonsensical. The production path (`executeAgent`) also has this issue but handles it differently — it falls back to "Analyze the recent releases" as the user prompt (line 325), but the system instruction still has empty placeholders.

The cleanest fix: when version is empty, use an alternative instruction that doesn't reference a specific version.

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go",
  "old_string": "func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {\n\tinstruction := DefaultInstruction\n\tif project.AgentPrompt != \"\" {\n\t\tinstruction = project.AgentPrompt + \"\\n\\n\" + instruction\n\t}\n\tinstruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, \"{{VERSION}}\", version)",
  "new_string": "func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {\n\tinstruction := DefaultInstruction\n\tif project.AgentPrompt != \"\" {\n\t\tinstruction = project.AgentPrompt + \"\\n\\n\" + instruction\n\t}\n\tif version != \"\" {\n\t\tinstruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, \"{{VERSION}}\", version)\n\t} else {\n\t\tinstruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, \"version {{VERSION}} of\", \"the latest releases of\")\n\t\tinstruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, \"{{VERSION}}\", \"the latest version\")\n\t}"

   ```

2. **`internal/agent/orchestrator.go`** — When version is empty, `{{VERSION}}` placeholders are replaced with sensible generic text ("the latest releases of" / "the latest version") instead of blank strings. > AGENT

The issue is in the search agent's description at line 132:

```
"Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources"
```

And the DefaultInstruction at line 55:

```
5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
```

Both explicitly tell the LLM to treat web search as a last resort. The root agent sees two tools — `data_agent` and `search_agent` — and the descriptions actively discourage search usage. The LLM reads "ONLY when" and almost never decides it's needed.

The fix is to make the instruction and description encourage search use rather than gatekeep it.

> AGENT

The two culprits:

1. **Line 55**: `"5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources"`
2. **Line 132**: `"Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues."`

Both use restrictive "ONLY when" phrasing that tells the LLM to treat search as a last resort. The fix is to make search a default step in the analysis workflow, not a conditional one.

> AGENT

Build passes. Here's what was changed and why:

**Root cause:** The `DefaultInstruction` and search agent `Description` both used "ONLY when" language, which tells the LLM to treat web search as a last resort. Since the root agent decides whether to delegate to sub-agents based on their descriptions and the system instruction, this phrasing caused it to almost never invoke `search_agent`.

**Fix (two lines in `orchestrator.go`):**

1. **Instruction (line 55):** Changed from _"Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources"_ → _"Always search the web for supplementary context about this release"_ — makes search a default step, not a conditional one.

2. **Search agent description (line 132):** Changed from _"Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources"_ → _"Use this for every release analysis to enrich the report with external insights"_ — tells the root agent to always delegate to search.

- **Should:** direct binary/artifact download URLs, GitHub/project release page URLs
- **Should not:** CVE links, security advisory URLs (nvd.nist.gov, osv.dev), blog posts, documentation

The previous instruction only said "prefer" direct downloads but never excluded non-download URLs, so the LLM treated any URL it found as fair game for that field.

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me compile the findings into a comprehensive report.

## Summary

I've completed a comprehensive search of the Changelogue codebase for agent tracing and logging mechanisms. Here are my findings:

---

## Key Findings

### 1. **Where `[agent_trace]` Log Messages Are Emitted**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` (lines 307-316)

```go
// Create the runner with logging plugin for agent trace visibility.
r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
    AppName:        "changelogue",
    Agent:          agentInstance,
    SessionService: sessionService,
    PluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{
        Plugins:      []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")},
        CloseTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
    },
})
```

The `[agent_trace]` logs are emitted by the ADK-Go **logging plugin** (`loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")`), which is part of the runner's plugin system. This plugin automatically logs to `slog` (Go's structured logging).

---

### 2. **How Sub-Agent Tool Calls Are Traced**

**Sub-agent creation is in lines 103-144 of the same file:**

```go
// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "data_agent",
    Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database...",
    Model:       llmModel,
    Tools:       functionTools,
})

// Search sub-agent: provider-specific web search.
searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "search_agent",
    Description: "Search the web for context about a release...",
    Model:       searchModel,
    Tools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},
})

// Root agent orchestrates sub-agents via agenttool
return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "release_analyst",
    Tools: []tool.Tool{
        agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),      // ← Second parameter is nil (no callback)
        agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),    // ← Second parameter is nil
    },
})
```

The sub-agents (`data_agent`, `search_agent`) are wrapped as tools using `agenttool.New()`. **The second parameter is `nil`**, meaning no callback mechanism for tracing internal sub-agent steps is currently configured.

---

### 3. **Event Loop and Tracing Limitation**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` (lines 331-350)

```go
var finalText string
eventCount := 0
for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
    if err != nil {
        slog.Error("agent: run event error",
            "run_id", run.ID,
            "event_count", eventCount,
            "err", err,
        )
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
    }
    eventCount++
    if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
        for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
            if part.Text != "" {
                finalText += part.Text
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**The problem:** The event loop only checks for `event.IsFinalResponse()` — it captures the final response from the root agent but **does not expose or process intermediate events** from sub-agent execution.

---

## Why Sub-Agent Internal Steps Aren't Traced

The ADK-Go framework's `agenttool.New()` function accepts a second parameter (currently `nil`) that would be a **callback or handler** to capture tool execution events. However:

1. **No callback is passed** — The code passes `nil` as the second parameter
2. **Event filtering is limited** — The main event loop only captures `IsFinalResponse()` events, which are from the root agent
3. **Nested agent visibility gap** — When the root agent calls a sub-agent tool, ADK-Go invokes that sub-agent internally, but the logging plugin and event stream don't propagate those internal steps back to the caller

---

## Architecture (from DESIGN.md)

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/DESIGN.md` (Section 5.4-5.6)

The system uses a 3-agent pattern:
- **Root agent** (`release_analyst`) — orchestrates the flow
- **Data sub-agent** (`data_agent`) — queries releases and context sources
- **Search sub-agent** (`search_agent`) — performs web search

Sub-agents are invoked as tools by the root agent. The logging plugin provides trace output to stdout/slog, but without explicit callbacks on sub-agent tool invocations, internal steps aren't captured.

---

## Relevant File Locations

| File | Lines | Purpose |
|------|-------|---------|
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | 103-156 | Sub-agent and tool creation |
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | 307-316 | Logging plugin configuration |
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | 331-350 | Event loop with trace filtering |
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go` | 91-122 | Function tools (data access) |
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/DESIGN.md` | 337-415 | Agent architecture design |
| `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/ARCH.md` | 165-187 | Agent layer implementation details |

---

## Conclusion

The trace shows sub-agent tool calls starting and completing because the logging plugin captures `agenttool` invocations at the **root agent level**. However, **what happens inside each sub-agent** (which tools it calls, what queries it makes, intermediate steps) is not exposed because:

1. No callback handler is registered with `agenttool.New()` 
2. The event loop only processes final responses, not intermediate tool calls from nested agents
3. The ADK-Go logging plugin logs to slog but doesn't provide structured access to nested agent execution details

To enable sub-agent internal tracing, you would need to either pass a callback function as the second parameter to `agenttool.New()` or enhance the event loop to process intermediate events from the runner's event stream.

    15→// Package agenttool provides a tool that allows an agent to call another agent.
    16→// This enables composition of agents, which can be useful for scenarios where
    17→// different types of `genai` tools cannot be used together.
    18→package agenttool
    19→

    21→	"encoding/json"

    38→// agentTool implements a tool that allows an agent to call another agent.
    39→type agentTool struct {
    40→	agent             agent.Agent
    41→	skipSummarization bool
    42→}
    43→
    44→// Config holds the configuration for an agent tool.
    45→type Config struct {
    46→	// SkipSummarization, if true, will cause the agent to skip summarization
    47→	// after the sub-agent finishes execution.
    48→	SkipSummarization bool
    49→}
    50→
    51→// New creates a new agent tool.
    52→// If cfg is nil, skipSummarization defaults to false.
    53→func New(agent agent.Agent, cfg *Config) tool.Tool {
    54→	if cfg == nil {
    55→		return &agentTool{
    56→			agent:             agent,
    57→			skipSummarization: false,
    58→		}
    59→	}
    60→	return &agentTool{
    61→		agent:             agent,
    62→		skipSummarization: cfg.SkipSummarization,
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// Name implements tool.Tool.
    67→func (t *agentTool) Name() string {
    68→	return t.agent.Name()
    69→}
    70→
    71→// Description implements tool.Tool.
    72→func (t *agentTool) Description() string {
    73→	return t.agent.Description()
    74→}
    75→
    76→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
    77→func (t *agentTool) IsLongRunning() bool {
    78→	return false
    79→}
    80→
    81→// Declaration returns the function declaration for the wrapped agent.
    82→// It generates a function declaration based on the agent's input schema.
    83→// If the agent does not have an input schema, a default schema with a
    84→// "request" string parameter is used.
    85→func (t *agentTool) Declaration() *genai.FunctionDeclaration {
    86→	decl := &genai.FunctionDeclaration{
    87→		Name:        t.Name(),
    88→		Description: t.Description(),
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	var agentInputSchema *genai.Schema
    92→	llmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
    93→	if ok && llmAgent != nil {
    94→		// TODO - understand what build_function_declaration does in python and apply if needed.
    95→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
    96→		if !ok {
    97→			return nil
    98→		}
    99→		agentInputSchema = llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).InputSchema
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	if agentInputSchema != nil {
   103→		decl.Parameters = agentInputSchema
   104→	} else {
   105→		decl.Parameters = &genai.Schema{
   106→			Type: "OBJECT",
   107→			Properties: map[string]*genai.Schema{
   108→				"request": {Type: "STRING"},
   109→			},
   110→			Required: []string{"request"},
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→	// TODO - understand how _api_variant affects response type.
   114→	return decl
   115→}
   116→
   117→// Run executes the wrapped agent with the provided arguments.
   118→// It creates a new session for the sub-agent, runs the agent, and returns
   119→// the final result.
   120→func (t *agentTool) Run(toolCtx tool.Context, args any) (map[string]any, error) {
   121→	margs, ok := args.(map[string]any)
   122→	if !ok {
   123→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agentTool expects map[string]any arguments, got %T", args)
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	if t.skipSummarization {
   127→		if actions := toolCtx.Actions(); actions != nil {
   128→			actions.SkipSummarization = true
   129→		}
   130→	}
   131→
   132→	var agentInputSchema *genai.Schema
   133→	llmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   134→	isLllmAgent := (ok && llmAgent != nil)
   135→	if isLllmAgent {
   136→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   137→		if !ok {
   138→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("internal error: failed to convert to llm agent")
   139→		}
   140→		agentInputSchema = llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).InputSchema
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	var content *genai.Content
   144→	var err error
   145→	if agentInputSchema != nil {
   146→		if err = utils.ValidateMapOnSchema(margs, agentInputSchema, true); err != nil {
   147→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("argument validation failed for agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   148→		}
   149→		jsonData, err := json.Marshal(margs)
   150→		if err != nil {
   151→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error serializing tool arguments for agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   152→		}
   153→		content = genai.NewContentFromText(string(jsonData), genai.RoleUser)
   154→	} else {
   155→		input, ok := margs["request"]
   156→		if !ok {
   157→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing required argument 'request' for agent %s", t.agent.Name())
   158→		}
   159→		inputText, ok := input.(string)
   160→		if !ok {
   161→			// Try to convert to string if not already one
   162→			inputText = fmt.Sprint(input)
   163→		}
   164→		content = genai.NewContentFromText(inputText, genai.RoleUser)
   165→	}
   166→
   167→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   168→
   169→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   170→		AppName:        t.agent.Name(),
   171→		Agent:          t.agent,
   172→		SessionService: sessionService,
   173→		// TODO - use forwarding_artifact_service as in python.
   174→		ArtifactService: artifact.InMemoryService(),
   175→		MemoryService:   memory.InMemoryService(),
   176→	})
   177→	if err != nil {
   178→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create runner")
   179→	}
   180→
   181→	stateMap := make(map[string]any)
   182→
   183→	for k, v := range toolCtx.State().All() {
   184→		// Filter out adk internal states.
   185→		if !strings.HasPrefix(k, "_adk") {
   186→			stateMap[k] = v
   187→		}
   188→	}
   189→
   190→	subSession, err := sessionService.Create(toolCtx, &session.CreateRequest{
   191→		AppName: t.agent.Name(),
   192→		UserID:  toolCtx.UserID(),
   193→		State:   stateMap,
   194→	})
   195→	if err != nil {
   196→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create session for sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	// TODO(dpasiukevich): verify agent loop termination.
   200→	eventCh := r.Run(toolCtx, subSession.Session.UserID(), subSession.Session.ID(), content, agent.RunConfig{
   201→		StreamingMode: agent.StreamingModeSSE,
   202→	})
   203→
   204→	var lastEvent *session.Event
   205→	for event, err := range eventCh {
   206→		if err != nil {
   207→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error during execution of sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   208→		}
   209→		if event.ErrorCode != "" || event.ErrorMessage != "" {
   210→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error from sub-agent %q (code: %q, message: %q)", t.agent.Name(), event.ErrorCode, event.ErrorMessage)
   211→		}
   212→		if event.LLMResponse.Content != nil {
   213→			lastEvent = event
   214→		}
   215→	}
   216→
   217→	if lastEvent == nil {
   218→		return map[string]any{}, nil
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	lastContent := lastEvent.LLMResponse.Content
   222→	var textParts []string
   223→	for _, part := range lastContent.Parts {
   224→		if part != nil && part.Text != "" {
   225→			textParts = append(textParts, part.Text)
   226→		}
   227→	}
   228→	outputText := strings.Join(textParts, "\n")
   229→
   230→	if outputText == "" {
   231→		return map[string]any{}, nil
   232→	}
   233→	if isLllmAgent {
   234→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   235→		if !ok {
   236→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("internal error: failed to convert to llm agent")
   237→		}
   238→		if agentOutputSchema := llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).OutputSchema; agentOutputSchema != nil {
   239→			// Assuming schemautils.ValidateOutputSchema parses the JSON string outputText
   240→			// and validates it against the agentOutputSchema, returning a map[string]any.
   241→			parsedOutput, err := utils.ValidateOutputSchema(outputText, agentOutputSchema)
   242→			if err != nil {
   243→				return nil, fmt.Errorf("output validation failed for sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   244→			}
   245→			return parsedOutput, nil
   246→		}
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	return map[string]any{"result": outputText}, nil
   250→}
   251→
   252→// ProcessRequest adds the agent tool's function declaration to the LLM request.
   253→func (t *agentTool) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   254→	// TODO extract this function somewhere else, simillar operations are done for
   255→	// other tools with function declaration.
   256→	if req.Tools == nil {
   257→		req.Tools = make(map[string]any)
   258→	}
   259→
   260→	name := t.Name()
   261→	if _, ok := req.Tools[name]; ok {
   262→		return fmt.Errorf("duplicate tool: %q", name)
   263→	}
   264→	req.Tools[name] = t
   265→
   266→	if req.Config == nil {
   267→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
   268→	}
   269→	if decl := t.Declaration(); decl == nil {
   270→		return nil
   271→	}
   272→	var funcTool *genai.Tool
   273→	for _, tool := range req.Config.Tools {
   274→		if tool != nil && tool.FunctionDeclarations != nil {
   275→			funcTool = tool
   276→			break
   277→		}
   278→	}
   279→	if funcTool == nil {
   280→		req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
   281→			FunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{t.Declaration()},
   282→		})
   283→	} else {
   284→		funcTool.FunctionDeclarations = append(funcTool.FunctionDeclarations, t.Declaration())
   285→	}
   286→	return nil
   287→}

Found it. In `agenttool`'s `Run` method (line 169), the sub-agent runner is created **without any plugins**:

```go
r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
    AppName:        t.agent.Name(),
    Agent:          t.agent,
    SessionService: sessionService,
    ArtifactService: artifact.InMemoryService(),
    MemoryService:   memory.InMemoryService(),
    // ← no PluginConfig — no logging plugin!
})
```

    28→// Session represents a series of interactions between a user and agents.
    29→//
    30→// When a user starts interacting with your agent, session holds everything
    31→// related to that one specific chat thread.
    32→type Session interface {
    33→	// ID returns the unique identifier of the session.
    34→	ID() string
    35→	// AppName returns name of the app.
    36→	AppName() string
    37→	// UserID returns the id of the user.
    38→	UserID() string
    39→
    40→	// State returns the state of the session.
    41→	State() State
    42→	// Events return the events of the session, e.g. user input, model response, function call/response, etc.
    43→	Events() Events
    44→	// LastUpdateTime returns the time of the last update.
    45→	LastUpdateTime() time.Time
    46→}
    47→
    48→// State defines a standard interface for a key-value store.
    49→// It provides basic methods for accessing, modifying, and iterating over
    50→// key-value pairs.
    51→type State interface {
    52→	// Get retrieves the value associated with a given key.
    53→	// It returns a ErrStateKeyNotExist error if the key does not exist.
    54→	Get(string) (any, error)
    55→	// Set assigns the given value to the given key, overwriting any
    56→	// existing value. It returns an error if the underlying storage
    57→	// operation fails.
    58→	Set(string, any) error
    59→	// All returns an iterator (iter.Seq2) that yields all key-value pairs
    60→	// currently in the state. The order of iteration is not guaranteed.
    61→	All() iter.Seq2[string, any]
    62→}
    63→
    64→// ReadonlyState defines a standard interface for a key-value store.
    65→// It provides basic methods for accessing, and iterating over
    66→// key-value pairs.
    67→type ReadonlyState interface {
    68→	// Get retrieves the value associated with a given key.
    69→	// It returns a ErrStateKeyNotExist error if the key does not exist.
    70→	Get(string) (any, error)
    71→	// All returns an iterator (iter.Seq2) that yields all key-value pairs
    72→	// currently in the state. The order of iteration is not guaranteed.
    73→	All() iter.Seq2[string, any]
    74→}
    75→
    76→// Events define a standard interface for an [Event] list.
    77→// It provides methods for iterating over the sequence and accessing
    78→// individual events by their index.
    79→type Events interface {
    80→	// All returns an iterator (iter.Seq) that yields all events
    81→	// in the sequence, preserving their order.
    82→	All() iter.Seq[*Event]
    83→	// Len returns the total number of events in the sequence.
    84→	Len() int
    85→	// At returns the event at the specified index i.
    86→	At(i int) *Event
    87→}
    88→
    89→// Event represents an interaction in a conversation between agents and users.
    90→// It is used to store the content of the conversation, as well as
    91→// the actions taken by the agents like function calls, etc.
    92→type Event struct {
    93→	model.LLMResponse
    94→
    95→	// Set by storage
    96→	ID        string
    97→	Timestamp time.Time
    98→
    99→	// Set by agent.Context implementation.
   100→	InvocationID string
   101→	// The branch of the event.
   102→	//
   103→	// The format is like agent_1.agent_2.agent_3, where agent_1 is
   104→	// the parent of agent_2, and agent_2 is the parent of agent_3.
   105→	//
   106→	// Branch is used when multiple sub-agent shouldn't see their peer agents'
   107→	// conversation history.
   108→	Branch string
   109→	// Author is the name of the event's author
   110→	Author string
   111→
   112→	// The actions taken by the agent.
   113→	Actions EventActions
   114→	// Set of IDs of the long running function calls.
   115→	// Agent client will know from this field about which function call is long running.
   116→	// Only valid for function call event.
   117→	LongRunningToolIDs []string
   118→}
   119→
   120→// IsFinalResponse returns whether the event is the final response of an agent.
   121→//
   122→// Note: when multiple agents participate in one invocation, there could be
   123→// multiple events with IsFinalResponse() as True, for each participating agent.
   124→func (e *Event) IsFinalResponse() bool {
   125→	if (e.Actions.SkipSummarization) || len(e.LongRunningToolIDs) > 0 {
   126→		return true
   127→	}
   128→
   129→	return !hasFunctionCalls(&e.LLMResponse) && !hasFunctionResponses(&e.LLMResponse) && !e.LLMResponse.Partial && !hasTrailingCodeExecutionResult(&e.LLMResponse)
   130→}
   131→
   132→// NewEvent creates a new event defining now as the timestamp.
   133→func NewEvent(invocationID string) *Event {
   134→	return &Event{
   135→		ID:           uuid.NewString(),
   136→		InvocationID: invocationID,
   137→		Timestamp:    time.Now(),
   138→		Actions:      EventActions{StateDelta: make(map[string]any)},
   139→	}
   140→}
   141→
   142→// EventActions represent the actions attached to an event.
   143→type EventActions struct {
   144→	// Set by agent.Context implementation.
   145→	StateDelta map[string]any
   146→
   147→	// Indicates that the event is updating an artifact. key is the filename,
   148→	// value is the version.
   149→	ArtifactDelta map[string]int64
   150→
   151→	RequestedToolConfirmations map[string]toolconfirmation.ToolConfirmation
   152→
   153→	// If true, it won't call model to summarize function response.
   154→	// Only valid for function response event.
   155→	SkipSummarization bool
   156→	// If set, the event transfers to the specified agent.
   157→	TransferToAgent string
   158→	// The agent is escalating to a higher level agent.
   159→	Escalate bool
   160→}
   161→
   162→// Prefixes for defining session's state scopes
   163→const (
   164→	// KeyPrefixApp is the prefix for app-level state keys.
   165→	// They are shared across all users and sessions for that application.
   166→	KeyPrefixApp string = "app:"
   167→	// KeyPrefixTemp is the prefix for temporary state keys.
   168→	// Such entries are specific to the current invocation (the entire process
   169→	// from an agent receiving user input to generating the final output for
   170→	// that input. Discarded after the invocation completes.
   171→	KeyPrefixTemp string = "temp:"
   172→	// KeyPrefixUser is the prefix for user-level state keys.
   173→	// They are tied to the user_id, shared across all sessions for that user
   174→	// (within the same app_name).
   175→	KeyPrefixUser string = "user:"
   176→)
   177→
   178→// ErrStateKeyNotExist is the error thrown when key does not exist.
   179→var ErrStateKeyNotExist = errors.New("state key does not exist")
   180→
   181→func hasFunctionCalls(resp *model.LLMResponse) bool {
   182→	if resp == nil || resp.Content == nil {
   183→		return false
   184→	}
   185→	for _, part := range resp.Content.Parts {
   186→		if part.FunctionCall != nil {
   187→			return true
   188→		}
   189→	}
   190→	return false
   191→}
   192→
   193→func hasFunctionResponses(resp *model.LLMResponse) bool {
   194→	if resp == nil || resp.Content == nil {
   195→		return false
   196→	}
   197→	for _, part := range resp.Content.Parts {
   198→		if part.FunctionResponse != nil {
   199→			return true
   200→		}
   201→	}
   202→	return false
   203→}
   204→
   205→// Returns whether the event has a trailing code execution result.
   206→func hasTrailingCodeExecutionResult(resp *model.LLMResponse) bool {
   207→	if resp == nil || resp.Content == nil || len(resp.Content.Parts) == 0 {
   208→		return false
   209→	}
   210→	lastPart := resp.Content.Parts[len(resp.Content.Parts)-1]
   211→	return lastPart.CodeExecutionResult != nil
   212→}
   213→

Now I see the full picture. The `Event` has an `Author` field and `Content` with `Parts` (which include `FunctionCall` and `FunctionResponse`). The event loop currently only processes `IsFinalResponse()` events. We can log all events — including tool calls and responses from sub-agents — by adding trace logging for every event.

Let me also check what the logging plugin does so I can replicate its format.

    15→package loggingplugin

    29→
    30→// New creates an instance of the logging plugin.
    31→//
    32→// This plugin helps print all critical events in the console. It is not a
    33→// replacement of existing logging in ADK. It rather helps terminal based
    34→// debugging by showing all logs in the console, and serves as a simple demo for
    35→// everyone to leverage when developing new plugins.
    36→//
    37→// This plugin helps users track the invocation status by logging:
    38→// - User messages and invocation context
    39→// - Agent execution flow
    40→// - LLM requests and responses
    41→// - Tool calls with arguments and results
    42→// - Events and final responses
    43→// - Errors during model and tool execution
    44→func New(name string) (*plugin.Plugin, error) {
    45→	if name == "" {
    46→		name = "logging_plugin"
    47→	}
    48→	p := &loggingPlugin{name: name}
    49→	return plugin.New(plugin.Config{
    50→		Name:                  name,
    51→		OnUserMessageCallback: p.onUserMessage,
    52→		BeforeRunCallback:     p.beforeRun,
    53→		OnEventCallback:       p.onEvent,
    54→		AfterRunCallback:      p.afterRun,
    55→		BeforeAgentCallback:   p.beforeAgent,
    56→		AfterAgentCallback:    p.afterAgent,
    57→		BeforeModelCallback:   p.beforeModel,
    58→		AfterModelCallback:    p.afterModel,
    59→		OnModelErrorCallback:  p.onModelError,
    60→		BeforeToolCallback:    p.beforeTool,
    61→		AfterToolCallback:     p.afterTool,
    62→		OnToolErrorCallback:   p.onToolError,
    63→	})
    64→}
    65→
    66→// MustNew is like New but panics if there is an error.
    67→func MustNew(name string) *plugin.Plugin {
    68→	p, err := New(name)
    69→	if err != nil {
    70→		panic(err)
    71→	}
    72→	return p
    73→}
    74→
    75→type loggingPlugin struct {
    76→	name string
    77→}
    78→
    79→func (p *loggingPlugin) log(msg string) {
    80→	// ANSI color codes: \033[90m for grey, \033[0m to reset
    81→	fmt.Printf("\033[90m[%s] %s\033[0m\n", p.name, msg)
    82→}
    83→
    84→func (p *loggingPlugin) formatContent(content *genai.Content, maxLength int) string {
    85→	if content == nil || len(content.Parts) == 0 {
    86→		return "None"
    87→	}
    88→
    89→	var parts []string
    90→	for _, part := range content.Parts {
    91→		if part.Text != "" {
    92→			text := strings.TrimSpace(part.Text)
    93→			if len(text) > maxLength {
    94→				text = text[:maxLength] + "..."
    95→			}
    96→			parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("text: '%s'", text))
    97→		} else if part.FunctionCall != nil {
    98→			parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("function_call: %s", part.FunctionCall.Name))
    99→		} else if part.FunctionResponse != nil {
   100→			parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("function_response: %s", part.FunctionResponse.Name))
   101→		} else if part.CodeExecutionResult != nil {
   102→			parts = append(parts, "code_execution_result")
   103→		} else {
   104→			parts = append(parts, "other_part")
   105→		}
   106→	}
   107→	return strings.Join(parts, " | ")
   108→}
   109→
   110→func (p *loggingPlugin) formatArgs(args map[string]any, maxLength int) string {
   111→	if len(args) == 0 {
   112→		return "{}"
   113→	}
   114→	formatted := fmt.Sprintf("%v", args)
   115→	if len(formatted) > maxLength {
   116→		formatted = formatted[:maxLength] + "...}"
   117→	}
   118→	return formatted
   119→}
