   121→func (p *loggingPlugin) onUserMessage(ctx agent.InvocationContext, userMessage *genai.Content) (*genai.Content, error) {
   122→	p.log("🚀 USER MESSAGE RECEIVED")
   123→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Invocation ID: %s", ctx.InvocationID()))
   124→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Session ID: %s", ctx.Session().ID()))
   125→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   User ID: %s", ctx.Session().UserID()))
   126→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   App Name: %s", ctx.Session().AppName()))
   127→	agentName := "Unknown"
   128→	if ctx.Agent() != nil {
   129→		agentName = ctx.Agent().Name()
   130→	}
   131→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Root Agent: %s", agentName))
   132→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   User Content: %s", p.formatContent(userMessage, 200)))
   133→	if ctx.Branch() != "" {
   134→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Branch: %s", ctx.Branch()))
   135→	}
   136→	return nil, nil
   137→}
   138→
   139→func (p *loggingPlugin) beforeRun(ctx agent.InvocationContext) (*genai.Content, error) {
   140→	p.log("🏃 INVOCATION STARTING")
   141→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Invocation ID: %s", ctx.InvocationID()))
   142→	agentName := "Unknown"
   143→	if ctx.Agent() != nil {
   144→		agentName = ctx.Agent().Name()
   145→	}
   146→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Starting Agent: %s", agentName))
   147→	return nil, nil
   148→}
   149→
   150→func (p *loggingPlugin) onEvent(ctx agent.InvocationContext, event *session.Event) (*session.Event, error) {
   151→	p.log("📢 EVENT YIELDED")
   152→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Event ID: %s", event.ID))
   153→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Author: %s", event.Author))
   154→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Content: %s", p.formatContent(event.Content, 200)))
   155→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Final Response: %v", event.IsFinalResponse()))
   156→
   157→	var funcCalls []string
   158→	var funcResponses []string
   159→
   160→	if event.Content != nil {
   161→		for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   162→			if part.FunctionCall != nil {
   163→				funcCalls = append(funcCalls, part.FunctionCall.Name)
   164→			}
   165→			if part.FunctionResponse != nil {
   166→				funcResponses = append(funcResponses, part.FunctionResponse.Name)
   167→			}
   168→		}
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	if len(funcCalls) > 0 {
   172→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Function Calls: %v", funcCalls))
   173→	}
   174→	if len(funcResponses) > 0 {
   175→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Function Responses: %v", funcResponses))
   176→	}
   177→	if len(event.LongRunningToolIDs) > 0 {
   178→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Long Running Tools: %v", event.LongRunningToolIDs))
   179→	}
   180→
   181→	return nil, nil
   182→}
   183→
   184→func (p *loggingPlugin) afterRun(ctx agent.InvocationContext) {
   185→	p.log("✅ INVOCATION COMPLETED")
   186→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Invocation ID: %s", ctx.InvocationID()))
   187→	agentName := "Unknown"
   188→	if ctx.Agent() != nil {
   189→		agentName = ctx.Agent().Name()
   190→	}
   191→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Final Agent: %s", agentName))
   192→}

   195→	p.log("🤖 AGENT STARTING")
   196→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Agent Name: %s", ctx.AgentName()))
   197→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Invocation ID: %s", ctx.InvocationID()))
   198→	if ctx.Branch() != "" {
   199→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Branch: %s", ctx.Branch()))
   200→	}
   201→	return nil, nil
   202→}
   203→
   204→func (p *loggingPlugin) afterAgent(ctx agent.CallbackContext) (*genai.Content, error) {
   205→	p.log("🤖 AGENT COMPLETED")
   206→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Agent Name: %s", ctx.AgentName()))
   207→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Invocation ID: %s", ctx.InvocationID()))
   208→	return nil, nil
   209→}

   211→func (p *loggingPlugin) beforeModel(ctx agent.CallbackContext, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   212→	p.log("🧠 LLM REQUEST")
   213→	modelName := "default"
   214→	if req.Model != "" {
   215→		modelName = req.Model
   216→	}
   217→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Model: %s", modelName))
   218→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Agent: %s", ctx.AgentName()))
   219→
   220→	if req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil && len(req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts) > 0 {
   221→		// Assuming SystemInstruction is a Content object with parts
   222→		sysInstruction := ""
   223→		for _, part := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {
   224→			sysInstruction += part.Text
   225→		}
   226→		if len(sysInstruction) > 200 {
   227→			sysInstruction = sysInstruction[:200] + "..."
   228→		}
   229→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   System Instruction: '%s'", sysInstruction))
   230→	}
   231→
   232→	if len(req.Tools) > 0 {
   233→		var toolNames []string
   234→		for name := range req.Tools {
   235→			toolNames = append(toolNames, name)
   236→		}
   237→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Available Tools: %v", toolNames))
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	return nil, nil
   241→}
   242→
   243→func (p *loggingPlugin) afterModel(ctx agent.CallbackContext, resp *model.LLMResponse, err error) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   244→	p.log("🧠 LLM RESPONSE")
   245→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Agent: %s", ctx.AgentName()))
   246→
   247→	// If error passed in, log it
   248→	if err != nil {
   249→		p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   ❌ ERROR - %v", err))
   250→		return nil, nil // Return nil, nil to propagate original error
   251→	}
   252→
   253→	if resp != nil {
   254→		if resp.ErrorCode != "" {
   255→			p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   ❌ ERROR - Code: %s", resp.ErrorCode))
   256→			p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Error Message: %s", resp.ErrorMessage))
   257→		} else {
   258→			p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Content: %s", p.formatContent(resp.Content, 200)))
   259→			if resp.Partial {
   260→				p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Partial: %v", resp.Partial))
   261→			}
   262→			// TurnComplete is a boolean in Go model
   263→			p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Turn Complete: %v", resp.TurnComplete))
   264→		}
   265→
   266→		if resp.UsageMetadata != nil {
   267→			p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Token Usage - Input: %d, Output: %d",
   268→				resp.UsageMetadata.PromptTokenCount, resp.UsageMetadata.CandidatesTokenCount))
   269→		}
   270→	}
   271→
   272→	return nil, nil
   273→}
   274→
   275→func (p *loggingPlugin) onModelError(ctx agent.CallbackContext, req *model.LLMRequest, err error) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   276→	p.log("🧠 LLM ERROR")
   277→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Agent: %s", ctx.AgentName()))
   278→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Error: %v", err))

   302→}
   303→
   304→func (p *loggingPlugin) onToolError(ctx tool.Context, t tool.Tool, args map[string]any, err error) (map[string]any, error) {

   310→	p.log(fmt.Sprintf("   Error: %v", err))

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. The logging plugin hooks into `BeforeToolCallback` and `AfterToolCallback` — these fire when the **root agent's runner** invokes a tool. So when the root agent calls `search_agent` or `data_agent` as tools, we see "TOOL STARTING" / "TOOL COMPLETED". 

But inside those tools, `agenttool.Run()` creates a **separate runner without plugins** (line 169 of `agent_tool.go`). That inner runner has no logging plugin, so the sub-agent's internal tool calls (e.g., `get_releases`, `web_search`) are invisible.

The fix: the `agenttool` doesn't support passing plugins. But we can instead **not use `agenttool`** for the sub-agents and instead manually run sub-agents through a runner that has the logging plugin. However, that's a huge refactor.

A simpler approach: we can look at whether the `runner.Config` has a way to propagate plugins. Let me check if there's a simpler option — the `plugin.Config` supports `BeforeToolCallback` which has access to the tool context. Since `agenttool.Run` creates its own runner, the only way to get tracing inside sub-agents is to modify how they're invoked.

Actually, the simplest fix: wrap the sub-agents' tools with logging. Instead of passing raw function tools to the sub-agents, we can wrap each tool to add logging. But that's also invasive.

Let me look at the actual trace output again — the user sees `TOOL STARTING` and `TOOL COMPLETED` for `search_agent` and `data_agent`, but not what happens inside them. The most pragmatic fix is to log all events from the main runner, not just final responses.

   159→// checkAllSourcesReady returns true if every source in the project has a
   160→// release matching the target version.
   161→func (o *Orchestrator) checkAllSourcesReady(ctx context.Context, projectID, version string) (bool, error) {
   162→	// Use a high limit to fetch all sources; projects rarely exceed a handful.
   163→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, 1, 1000)
   164→	if err != nil {
   165→		return false, fmt.Errorf("list sources: %w", err)
   166→	}
   167→	if len(sources) == 0 {
   168→		return true, nil
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	for _, src := range sources {
   172→		has, err := o.store.HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, src.ID, version)
   173→		if err != nil {
   174→			return false, fmt.Errorf("check source %s: %w", src.ID, err)
   175→		}
   176→		if !has {
   177→			slog.Info("agent: source not ready for version",
   178→				"project_id", projectID,
   179→				"source_id", src.ID,
   180→				"version", version,
   181→			)
   182→			return false, nil
   183→		}
   184→	}
   185→	return true, nil
   186→}

   188→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
   189→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
   190→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
   191→type Orchestrator struct {
   192→	store     OrchestratorStore
   193→	llmConfig LLMConfig
   194→	publicURL string // base URL for internal Changelogue links (e.g. "https://changelogue.example.com")
   195→}
   196→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tracedagenttool.go",
  "content": "package agent\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/genai\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/agent\"\n\tllmagent \"google.golang.org/adk/agent/llmagent\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/model\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/plugin\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/plugin/loggingplugin\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/runner\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/session\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/adk/tool\"\n)\n\n// tracedAgentTool wraps a sub-agent as a tool (like agenttool.New) but attaches\n// the logging plugin to the sub-agent's runner so that internal tool calls and\n// LLM interactions are visible in the trace output.\ntype tracedAgentTool struct {\n\tagent agent.Agent\n}\n\n// newTracedAgentTool creates a new traced agent tool.\nfunc newTracedAgentTool(a agent.Agent) tool.Tool {\n\treturn &tracedAgentTool{agent: a}\n}\n\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) Name() string        { return t.agent.Name() }\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) Description() string  { return t.agent.Description() }\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) IsLongRunning() bool  { return false }\n\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) Declaration() *genai.FunctionDeclaration {\n\treturn &genai.FunctionDeclaration{\n\t\tName:        t.Name(),\n\t\tDescription: t.Description(),\n\t\tParameters: &genai.Schema{\n\t\t\tType: \"OBJECT\",\n\t\t\tProperties: map[string]*genai.Schema{\n\t\t\t\t\"request\": {Type: \"STRING\"},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tRequired: []string{\"request\"},\n\t\t},\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) Run(toolCtx tool.Context, args any) (map[string]any, error) {\n\tmargs, ok := args.(map[string]any)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"tracedAgentTool expects map[string]any arguments, got %T\", args)\n\t}\n\n\tinput, ok := margs[\"request\"]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"missing required argument 'request' for agent %s\", t.agent.Name())\n\t}\n\tinputText, ok := input.(string)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tinputText = fmt.Sprint(input)\n\t}\n\tcontent := genai.NewContentFromText(inputText, genai.RoleUser)\n\n\tsessionService := session.InMemoryService()\n\n\tr, err := runner.New(runner.Config{\n\t\tAppName:        t.agent.Name(),\n\t\tAgent:          t.agent,\n\t\tSessionService: sessionService,\n\t\tPluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{\n\t\t\tPlugins: []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew(\"agent_trace\")},\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create runner for sub-agent %s: %w\", t.agent.Name(), err)\n\t}\n\n\tstateMap := make(map[string]any)\n\tfor k, v := range toolCtx.State().All() {\n\t\tif !strings.HasPrefix(k, \"_adk\") {\n\t\t\tstateMap[k] = v\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tsubSession, err := sessionService.Create(toolCtx, &session.CreateRequest{\n\t\tAppName: t.agent.Name(),\n\t\tUserID:  toolCtx.UserID(),\n\t\tState:   stateMap,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create session for sub-agent %s: %w\", t.agent.Name(), err)\n\t}\n\n\teventCh := r.Run(toolCtx, subSession.Session.UserID(), subSession.Session.ID(), content, agent.RunConfig{})\n\n\tvar lastEvent *session.Event\n\tfor event, err := range eventCh {\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"error during execution of sub-agent %s: %w\", t.agent.Name(), err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif event.ErrorCode != \"\" || event.ErrorMessage != \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"error from sub-agent %q (code: %q, message: %q)\", t.agent.Name(), event.ErrorCode, event.ErrorMessage)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif event.LLMResponse.Content != nil {\n\t\t\tlastEvent = event\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif lastEvent == nil {\n\t\treturn map[string]any{}, nil\n\t}\n\n\tlastContent := lastEvent.LLMResponse.Content\n\tvar textParts []string\n\tfor _, part := range lastContent.Parts {\n\t\tif part != nil && part.Text != \"\" {\n\t\t\ttextParts = append(textParts, part.Text)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\toutputText := strings.Join(textParts, \"\\n\")\n\tif outputText == \"\" {\n\t\treturn map[string]any{}, nil\n\t}\n\treturn map[string]any{\"result\": outputText}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (t *tracedAgentTool) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {\n\tif req.Tools == nil {\n\t\treq.Tools = make(map[string]any)\n\t}\n\tname := t.Name()\n\tif _, ok := req.Tools[name]; ok {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"duplicate tool: %q\", name)\n\t}\n\treq.Tools[name] = t\n\n\tif req.Config == nil {\n\t\treq.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}\n\t}\n\tdecl := t.Declaration()\n\tif decl == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tvar funcTool *genai.Tool\n\tfor _, gt := range req.Config.Tools {\n\t\tif gt != nil && gt.FunctionDeclarations != nil {\n\t\t\tfuncTool = gt\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif funcTool == nil {\n\t\treq.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{\n\t\t\tFunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{decl},\n\t\t})\n\t} else {\n\t\tfuncTool.FunctionDeclarations = append(funcTool.FunctionDeclarations, decl)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n"
}

    45→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    46→
    47→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    48→
    49→Use the available tools to:
    50→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    51→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    52→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    53→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    54→5. Always search the web for supplementary context about this release
    55→   (community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues, migration guides).
    56→
    57→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    58→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    59→
    60→For download_links: ONLY include URLs where the user can actually download the software — direct binary/artifact URLs for specific platforms (e.g., linux-amd64, darwin-arm64, windows-amd64 .tar.gz/.zip files) and the GitHub/project release page. Do NOT include CVE links, security advisory URLs, blog posts, documentation, or any non-download URLs.
    61→
    62→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    63→{
    64→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Urgency Summary>)",
    65→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    66→  "urgency_reason": "Why this urgency level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    67→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    68→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    69→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    70→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    71→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    72→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    73→  "download_links": ["https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-linux-amd64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-darwin-arm64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    74→}`

> AGENT

`AgentDataStore` doesn't have `ListSourcesByProject`, but `BuildAgent` takes the `store` as `AgentDataStore`. However, the `OrchestratorStore` (which embeds `AgentDataStore`) does have `ListSourcesByProject`. 

The simplest approach: `BuildAgent` already receives the `project` with a name. For the search instruction, I can fetch the sources inside `BuildAgent` by accepting a list of source summaries. But `BuildAgent` only takes `AgentDataStore`. Let me keep it simple — the orchestrator can pass source info into `BuildAgent`, or I can add `ListSourcesByProject` to `AgentDataStore`. Actually, the simplest: read the sources in the orchestrator (which has `OrchestratorStore`) and pass them into the search instruction via the project or a new parameter.

> AGENT

Both `cmd/agent/main.go` and the orchestrator call `BuildAgent`. The `store` in `cmd/agent/main.go` is `api.NewPgStore` which implements `OrchestratorStore` (and thus `AgentDataStore`). But `BuildAgent` only accepts `AgentDataStore`.

The simplest approach: add `ListSourcesByProject` to `AgentDataStore`, or just pass the sources as a separate parameter. Adding to the interface is cleaner since both callers have stores that implement it.

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's a summary of the three fixes in this change:

**1. Sub-agent trace visibility** — Created `tracedagenttool.go` that replaces `agenttool.New()`. The upstream `agenttool` creates sub-agent runners without plugins, so internal tool calls (DB queries, web searches) were invisible in traces. The new wrapper injects the `loggingplugin` into sub-agent runners.

**2. Search agent project context** — The search agent now receives a system instruction with the project name **and** its upstream source URLs/providers (e.g. `- github: ethereum/go-ethereum`). This prevents ambiguous "v3.0.8" searches that return results for unrelated projects.

**3. Source interface consolidation** — Moved `ListSourcesByProject` from `OrchestratorStore` into `AgentDataStore` so `BuildAgent` can fetch sources without requiring the full orchestrator store.

    13→// AgentDataStore defines the data access methods that agent tools use to
    14→// research releases and context sources for a given project.
    15→type AgentDataStore interface {
    16→	ListReleasesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int, includeExcluded bool) ([]models.Release, int, error)
    17→	GetRelease(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Release, error)
    18→	ListContextSources(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.ContextSource, int, error)
    19→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    20→}
    21→
    22→// --- Tool input/output types ---
    23→
    24→// GetReleasesInput is the input for the get_releases tool.
    25→type GetReleasesInput struct {
    26→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    27→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    28→}
    29→
    30→// ReleaseItem is a single release in the tool output.
    31→type ReleaseItem struct {
    32→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    33→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    34→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    35→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    36→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    37→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    38→}
    39→
    40→// GetReleasesOutput is the output for the get_releases tool.
    41→type GetReleasesOutput struct {
    42→	Releases []ReleaseItem `json:"releases"`
    43→	Total    int           `json:"total"`
    44→	Page     int           `json:"page"`
    45→	PerPage  int           `json:"per_page"`
    46→}
    47→
    48→// GetReleaseDetailInput is the input for the get_release_detail tool.
    49→type GetReleaseDetailInput struct {
    50→	ReleaseID string `json:"release_id"`
    51→}
    52→
    53→// GetReleaseDetailOutput is the output for the get_release_detail tool.
    54→type GetReleaseDetailOutput struct {
    55→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    56→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    57→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    58→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    59→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    60→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    61→}
    62→
    63→// ListContextSourcesInput is the input for the list_context_sources tool.
    64→type ListContextSourcesInput struct {
    65→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    66→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    67→}
    68→
    69→// ContextSourceItem is a single context source in the tool output.
    70→type ContextSourceItem struct {
    71→	ID     string `json:"id"`
    72→	Type   string `json:"type"`
    73→	Name   string `json:"name"`
    74→	Config any    `json:"config"`
    75→}
    76→
    77→// ListContextSourcesOutput is the output for the list_context_sources tool.
    78→type ListContextSourcesOutput struct {
    79→	Sources []ContextSourceItem `json:"sources"`
    80→	Total   int                 `json:"total"`
    81→	Page    int                 `json:"page"`
    82→	PerPage int                 `json:"per_page"`
    83→}
    84→
    85→// toolFactory holds the store reference and project ID scoped to a single
    86→// agent run. Tools created from this factory are bound to a specific project.
    87→type toolFactory struct {
    88→	store     AgentDataStore
    89→	projectID string
    90→}
    91→
    92→// NewTools creates the ADK function tools for agent research, scoped to a
    93→// specific project. The returned tools use the provided store to query
    94→// releases and context sources belonging to projectID.
    95→func NewTools(store AgentDataStore, projectID string) ([]tool.Tool, error) {
    96→	f := &toolFactory{store: store, projectID: projectID}
    97→
    98→	getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
    99→		Name:        "get_releases",
   100→		Description: "Fetch recent releases for the current project. Returns a paginated list of releases with version, raw data, and timestamps. Use this to understand what new versions have been published.",
   101→	}, f.getReleases)
   102→	if err != nil {
   103→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_releases tool: %w", err)
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	getReleaseDetail, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   107→		Name:        "get_release_detail",
   108→		Description: "Get detailed information for a specific release by its ID. Returns the full raw data payload from the upstream provider. Use this to inspect changelogs, commit lists, or other release metadata.",
   109→	}, f.getReleaseDetail)
   110→	if err != nil {
   111→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_release_detail tool: %w", err)
   112→	}
   113→
   114→	listContextSources, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   115→		Name:        "list_context_sources",
   116→		Description: "List the context sources configured for this project. Context sources are references to runbooks, documentation, monitoring dashboards, and other background materials that help you produce richer analysis.",
   117→	}, f.listContextSources)
   118→	if err != nil {
   119→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create list_context_sources tool: %w", err)
   120→	}
   121→
   122→	return []tool.Tool{getReleases, getReleaseDetail, listContextSources}, nil
   123→}
   124→
   125→// getReleases is the handler for the get_releases tool.
   126→func (f *toolFactory) getReleases(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleasesInput) (GetReleasesOutput, error) {
   127→	page := input.Page
   128→	if page < 1 {
   129→		page = 1
   130→	}
   131→	perPage := input.PerPage
   132→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   133→		perPage = 20
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	releases, total, err := f.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage, false)
   137→	if err != nil {
   138→		return GetReleasesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list releases: %w", err)
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	items := make([]ReleaseItem, 0, len(releases))
   142→	for _, r := range releases {
   143→		item := ReleaseItem{
   144→			ID:        r.ID,
   145→			SourceID:  r.SourceID,
   146→			Version:   r.Version,
   147→			RawData:   r.RawData,
   148→			CreatedAt: r.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   149→		}
   150→		if r.ReleasedAt != nil {
   151→			item.ReleasedAt = r.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   152→		}
   153→		items = append(items, item)
   154→	}
   155→
   156→	return GetReleasesOutput{
   157→		Releases: items,
   158→		Total:    total,
   159→		Page:     page,
   160→		PerPage:  perPage,
   161→	}, nil
   162→}
   163→
   164→// getReleaseDetail is the handler for the get_release_detail tool.
   165→func (f *toolFactory) getReleaseDetail(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleaseDetailInput) (GetReleaseDetailOutput, error) {
   166→	if input.ReleaseID == "" {
   167→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("release_id is required")
   168→	}
   169→
   170→	release, err := f.store.GetRelease(ctx, input.ReleaseID)
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("get release: %w", err)
   173→	}
   174→
   175→	out := GetReleaseDetailOutput{
   176→		ID:        release.ID,
   177→		SourceID:  release.SourceID,
   178→		Version:   release.Version,
   179→		RawData:   release.RawData,
   180→		CreatedAt: release.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   181→	}
   182→	if release.ReleasedAt != nil {
   183→		out.ReleasedAt = release.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   184→	}
   185→
   186→	return out, nil
   187→}
   188→
   189→// listContextSources is the handler for the list_context_sources tool.
   190→func (f *toolFactory) listContextSources(ctx tool.Context, input ListContextSourcesInput) (ListContextSourcesOutput, error) {
   191→	page := input.Page
   192→	if page < 1 {
   193→		page = 1
   194→	}
   195→	perPage := input.PerPage
   196→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   197→		perPage = 20
   198→	}
   199→
   200→	sources, total, err := f.store.ListContextSources(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage)
   201→	if err != nil {
   202→		return ListContextSourcesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list context sources: %w", err)
   203→	}
   204→
   205→	items := make([]ContextSourceItem, 0, len(sources))
   206→	for _, s := range sources {
   207→		items = append(items, ContextSourceItem{

The issue is clear. The `GetReleaseDetailInput` struct has `ReleaseID string \`json:"release_id"\`` but the ADK `functiontool` generates the JSON schema from the struct, and the `release_id` field has no `required` tag or indication to the LLM that it's mandatory — the schema is inferred from the Go struct.     33→// FunctionTool: borrow implementation from MCP go.

    35→// Config is the input to the NewFunctionTool function.
    36→type Config struct {
    37→	// The name of this tool.
    38→	Name string
    39→	// A human-readable description of the tool.
    40→	Description string
    41→	// An optional JSON schema object defining the expected parameters for the tool.
    42→	// If it is nil, FunctionTool tries to infer the schema based on the handler type.
    43→	InputSchema *jsonschema.Schema
    44→	// An optional JSON schema object defining the structure of the tool's output.
    45→	// If it is nil, FunctionTool tries to infer the schema based on the handler type.
    46→	OutputSchema *jsonschema.Schema
    47→	// IsLongRunning makes a FunctionTool a long-running operation.
    48→	IsLongRunning bool
    49→
    50→	// RequireConfirmation flags whether this tool must always ask for user confirmation
    51→	// before execution. If set to true, the ADK framework will automatically initiate
    52→	// a Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) confirmation request when this tool is invoked.
    53→	RequireConfirmation bool
    54→
    55→	// RequireConfirmationProvider allows for dynamic determination of whether
    56→	// user confirmation is needed. This field is a function called at runtime to decide if
    57→	// a confirmation request should be sent. The function takes the tool's input parameters as arguments.
    58→	// This provider offers more flexibility than the static RequireConfirmation flag,
    59→	// enabling conditional confirmation based on the invocation details.
    60→	// If set, this often takes precedence over the RequireConfirmation flag.
    61→	//
    62→	// Required signature for a provider function:
    63→	// func(toolInput ToolArgs) (bool)
    64→	// where ToolArgs is the input type of your go function
    65→	// Returning true means confirmation is required.
    66→	RequireConfirmationProvider any
    67→}
    68→
    69→// Func represents a Go function that can be wrapped in a tool.
    70→// It takes a tool.Context and a generic argument type, and returns a generic result type.
    71→type Func[TArgs, TResults any] func(tool.Context, TArgs) (TResults, error)
    72→
    73→// ErrInvalidArgument indicates the input parameter type is invalid.
    74→var ErrInvalidArgument = errors.New("invalid argument")
    75→
    76→// New creates a new tool with a name, description, and the provided handler.
    77→// Input schema is automatically inferred from the input and output types.
    78→func New[TArgs, TResults any](cfg Config, handler Func[TArgs, TResults]) (tool.Tool, error) {
    79→	// TODO: How can we improve UX for functions that does not require an argument, returns a simple type value, or returns a no result?
    80→	// https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/discussions/37
    81→
    82→	var zeroArgs TArgs
    83→	argsType := reflect.TypeOf(zeroArgs)
    84→	for argsType != nil && argsType.Kind() == reflect.Ptr {
    85→		argsType = argsType.Elem()
    86→	}
    87→	if argsType == nil || (argsType.Kind() != reflect.Struct && argsType.Kind() != reflect.Map) {
    88→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("input must be a struct or a map or a pointer to those types, but received: %v: %w", argsType, ErrInvalidArgument)
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	ischema, err := resolvedSchema[TArgs](cfg.InputSchema)
    92→	if err != nil {
    93→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to infer input schema: %w", err)
    94→	}
    95→	oschema, err := resolvedSchema[TResults](cfg.OutputSchema)
    96→	if err != nil {
    97→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to infer output schema: %w", err)
    98→	}
    99→
   100→	var confirmWrapper func(TArgs) bool
   101→
   102→	if cfg.RequireConfirmationProvider != nil {
   103→		// Attempt to cast the interface directly to the function signature
   104→		fn, ok := cfg.RequireConfirmationProvider.(func(TArgs) bool)
   105→		if !ok {
   106→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error RequireConfirmationProvider must be a function with signature func(%T) bool", *new(TArgs))
   107→		}
   108→		confirmWrapper = fn
   109→	}
   110→
   111→	return &functionTool[TArgs, TResults]{
   112→		cfg:                         cfg,
   113→		inputSchema:                 ischema,
   114→		outputSchema:                oschema,
   115→		handler:                     handler,
   116→		requireConfirmation:         cfg.RequireConfirmation,
   117→		requireConfirmationProvider: confirmWrapper,
   118→	}, nil
   119→}
   120→
   121→// functionTool wraps a Go function.
   122→type functionTool[TArgs, TResults any] struct {
   123→	cfg Config
   124→
   125→	// A JSON Schema object defining the expected parameters for the tool.
   126→	inputSchema *jsonschema.Resolved
   127→	// A JSON Schema object defining the result of the tool.
   128→	outputSchema *jsonschema.Resolved
   129→
   130→	// handler is the Go function.
   131→	handler Func[TArgs, TResults]
   132→
   133→	requireConfirmation bool
   134→
   135→	requireConfirmationProvider func(TArgs) bool
   136→}
   137→
   138→// Description implements tool.Tool.
   139→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) Description() string {
   140→	return f.cfg.Description
   141→}
   142→
   143→// Name implements tool.Tool.
   144→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) Name() string {
   145→	return f.cfg.Name
   146→}
   147→
   148→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
   149→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) IsLongRunning() bool {
   150→	return f.cfg.IsLongRunning
   151→}
   152→
   153→// ProcessRequest packs the function tool's declaration into the LLM request.
   154→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   155→	return toolutils.PackTool(req, f)
   156→}
   157→
   158→// FunctionDeclaration implements interfaces.FunctionTool.
   159→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) Declaration() *genai.FunctionDeclaration {
   160→	decl := &genai.FunctionDeclaration{
   161→		Name:        f.Name(),
   162→		Description: f.Description(),
   163→	}
   164→	if f.inputSchema != nil {
   165→		decl.ParametersJsonSchema = f.inputSchema.Schema()
   166→	}
   167→	if f.outputSchema != nil {
   168→		decl.ResponseJsonSchema = f.outputSchema.Schema()
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	if f.cfg.IsLongRunning {
   172→		instruction := "NOTE: This is a long-running operation. Do not call this tool again if it has already returned some intermediate or pending status."
   173→		if decl.Description != "" {
   174→			decl.Description += "\n\n" + instruction
   175→		} else {
   176→			decl.Description = instruction
   177→		}
   178→	}
   179→
   180→	return decl
   181→}
   182→
   183→// Run executes the tool with the provided context and yields events.
   184→func (f *functionTool[TArgs, TResults]) Run(ctx tool.Context, args any) (result map[string]any, err error) {
   185→	// TODO: Handle function call request from tc.InvocationContext.
   186→	defer func() {
   187→		if r := recover(); r != nil {
   188→			err = fmt.Errorf("panic in tool %q: %v\nstack: %s", f.Name(), r, debug.Stack())
   189→		}
   190→	}()
   191→
   192→	m, ok := args.(map[string]any)
   193→	if !ok {
   194→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected args type, got: %T", args)
   195→	}
   196→	input, err := typeutil.ConvertToWithJSONSchema[map[string]any, TArgs](m, f.inputSchema)
   197→	if err != nil {
   198→		return nil, err
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	if confirmation := ctx.ToolConfirmation(); confirmation != nil {
   202→		if !confirmation.Confirmed {
   203→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error tool %q call is rejected", f.Name())
   204→		}
   205→	} else {
   206→		requireConfirmation := f.requireConfirmation
   207→
   208→		// Only run the potentially expensive provider if the static flag didn't already trigger it
   209→		// Provider takes precedence/overrides:
   210→		if f.requireConfirmationProvider != nil {
   211→			requireConfirmation = f.requireConfirmationProvider(input)
   212→		}
   213→
   214→		if requireConfirmation {
   215→			err := ctx.RequestConfirmation(
   216→				fmt.Sprintf("Please approve or reject the tool call %s() by responding with a FunctionResponse with an expected ToolConfirmation payload.",
   217→					f.Name()), nil)
   218→			if err != nil {
   219→				return nil, err
   220→			}
   221→			ctx.Actions().SkipSummarization = true
   222→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error tool %q requires confirmation, please approve or reject", f.Name())
   223→		}
   224→	}
   225→
   226→	output, err := f.handler(ctx, input)
   227→	if err != nil {
   228→		return nil, err
   229→	}
   230→	resp, err := typeutil.ConvertToWithJSONSchema[TResults, map[string]any](output, f.outputSchema)
   231→	if err == nil { // all good
   232→		return resp, nil
   233→	}
   234→
   235→	// Specs requires the result to be a map (dict in python). python impl allows basic types when building response event
   236→	// functions.py __build_response_event does the following
   237→	// if not isinstance(function_result, dict):
   238→	// 		function_result = {'result': function_result}
   239→	if f.outputSchema != nil {
   240→		if err1 := f.outputSchema.Validate(output); err1 != nil {
   241→			return resp, err // if it fails propagate original err.
   242→		}
   243→	}
   244→	wrappedOutput := map[string]any{"result": output}
   245→	return wrappedOutput, nil
   246→}
   247→
   248→// ** NOTE FOR REVIEWERS **
   249→// Initially I started to borrow the design of the MCP ServerTool and
   250→// ToolHandlerFor/ToolHandler [1], but got diverged.
   251→//  * MCP ServerTool provides direct access to mcp.CallToolResult message
   252→//    but we expect Function in our case is a simple wrapper around a Go
   253→//    function, and does not need to worry about how the result is translated
   254→//    in genai.Content.
   255→//  * Function returns only TResults, not (TResults, error). If the user
   256→//    function can return an error, that needs to be included in the output
   257→//    json schema. And for function that never returns an error, I think it
   258→//    gets less uglier.
   259→//  * MCP ToolHandler expects mcp.ServerSession. types.ToolContext may be close
   260→//    to it, but we don't need to expose this to user function
   261→//    (similar to ADK Python FunctionTool [2])
   262→// References
   263→//  [1] MCP SDK https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk@v0.0.0-20250625213837-ff0d746521c4/mcp#ToolHandler
   264→//  [2] ADK Python https://github.REDACTED.py#L110-L112
   265→
   266→func resolvedSchema[T any](override *jsonschema.Schema) (*jsonschema.Resolved, error) {
   267→	// TODO: check if override schema is compatible with T.
   268→	if override != nil {
   269→		return override.Resolve(nil)
   270→	}
   271→	schema, err := jsonschema.For[T](nil)
   272→	if err != nil {
   273→		return nil, err
   274→	}
   275→	return schema.Resolve(nil)
   276→}

> AGENT

I see — the `functiontool` uses `jsonschema.For[T]` to generate the schema from the struct, and the validation happens via `typeutil.ConvertToWithJSONSchema` which validates against the resolved schema. The error `required: missing properties: ["release_id"]` means the schema correctly marks `release_id` as required, but the LLM sends `{}`. 

The problem is the OpenAI Responses API adapter. The LLM **is** sending `release_id` but it's likely being lost in the conversion. Let me check how the `schemaToMap` function handles `Required` fields — the OpenAI adapter uses `schemaToMap` to convert `genai.Schema` to JSON for the API, but `functiontool` uses `ParametersJsonSchema` (raw JSON schema) not `Parameters` (genai.Schema).

Let me check this more carefully.

   267→// convertResponseTools transforms ADK tools to Responses API flat format.
   268→// Function tools become {type: "function", name, description, parameters}.
   269→// If a GoogleSearch marker is present, a {type: "web_search"} tool is added
   270→// using wsConfig for search_context_size and allowed_domains.
   271→func convertResponseTools(req *model.LLMRequest, wsConfig *WebSearch) []responseTool {
   272→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   273→		return nil
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	var tools []responseTool
   277→	hasSearch := false
   278→
   279→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   280→		if t.GoogleSearch != nil {
   281→			hasSearch = true
   282→			continue
   283→		}
   284→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   285→			rt := responseTool{
   286→				Type:        "function",
   287→				Name:        fd.Name,
   288→				Description: fd.Description,
   289→			}
   290→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   291→				rt.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   292→			}
   293→			tools = append(tools, rt)
   294→		}
   295→	}
   296→
   297→	if hasSearch {
   298→		wsTool := responseTool{Type: "web_search"}
   299→		if wsConfig != nil {
   300→			wsTool.SearchContextSize = wsConfig.SearchContextSize
   301→			if len(wsConfig.AllowedDomains) > 0 {
   302→				wsTool.Filters = &searchFilters{
   303→					AllowedDomains: wsConfig.AllowedDomains,
   304→				}
   305→			}
   306→		}
   307→		tools = append(tools, wsTool)
   308→	}
   309→
   310→	return tools
   311→}

> AGENT

There it is. Line 290: `if fd.Parameters != nil` — this checks `fd.Parameters` (a `*genai.Schema`), but the `functiontool` sets `fd.ParametersJsonSchema` (raw JSON schema), not `fd.Parameters`. So the parameters are **never sent to OpenAI** — the LLM sees tools with no parameter schema and doesn't know what arguments to pass.

> AGENT

Confirmed. The `functiontool` sets `decl.ParametersJsonSchema` (type `*jsonschema.Schema`), not `decl.Parameters` (type `*genai.Schema`). Our OpenAI adapter only checks `fd.Parameters` and uses `schemaToMap` to convert `*genai.Schema` — it completely ignores `fd.ParametersJsonSchema`.

The fix: in `convertResponseTools`, also check `fd.ParametersJsonSchema` and serialize it directly (it's already JSON schema format, no conversion needed).

> AGENT

All passing. Here's the root cause and fix:

**Root cause:** The ADK `functiontool` sets `fd.ParametersJsonSchema` (raw JSON schema) on function declarations, **not** `fd.Parameters` (genai.Schema). Our OpenAI Responses API adapter only checked `fd.Parameters`, so **all function tool parameters were dropped** — the LLM saw tools with no schema and had to guess at the arguments, leading to empty `{}` calls.

**Fix:** `convertResponseTools` now checks `fd.ParametersJsonSchema` first (pass through directly since it's already JSON schema), falling back to `fd.Parameters` + `schemaToMap()` for tools that use the older genai.Schema format.

> DEVELOPER

The simplest and correct fix: use `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` (upsert) in the SQL so a re-run for the same project+version updates the existing report instead of failing.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/pgstore.go",
  "old_string": "// CreateSemanticRelease inserts a semantic release and its source release links\n// in a single transaction. The sr.ID field is populated on success.\nfunc (s *PgStore) CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error {\n\ttx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"begin tx: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer tx.Rollback(ctx)\n\n\terr = tx.QueryRow(ctx,\n\t\t`INSERT INTO semantic_releases (project_id, version, report, status, completed_at)\n\t\t VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n\t\t RETURNING id, created_at`,\n\t\tsr.ProjectID, sr.Version, sr.Report, sr.Status, sr.CompletedAt,\n\t).Scan(&sr.ID, &sr.CreatedAt)",
  "new_string": "// CreateSemanticRelease upserts a semantic release and its source release links\n// in a single transaction. If a semantic release for the same project+version\n// already exists, the report, status, and completed_at are updated.\n// The sr.ID field is populated on success.\nfunc (s *PgStore) CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error {\n\ttx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"begin tx: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer tx.Rollback(ctx)\n\n\terr = tx.QueryRow(ctx,\n\t\t`INSERT INTO semantic_releases (project_id, version, report, status, completed_at)\n\t\t VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n\t\t ON CONFLICT (project_id, version) DO UPDATE\n\t\t SET report = EXCLUDED.report, status = EXCLUDED.status, completed_at = EXCLUDED.completed_at\n\t\t RETURNING id, created_at`,\n\t\tsr.ProjectID, sr.Version, sr.Report, sr.Status, sr.CompletedAt,\n\t).Scan(&sr.ID, &sr.CreatedAt)"
}

All passing. The fix: changed the `INSERT` to `ON CONFLICT (project_id, version) DO UPDATE` so re-running the agent for the same project+version updates the existing semantic release instead of failing with a duplicate key error.
